#### 7.4.3.10 Local Iwasawa decomposition for normalized induction

A smooth induced section is determined on a compact subgroup once each group element can be moved there by left multiplication by the parabolic. At a prescribed right level this can turn a function on the entire group into a finite list of values. To obtain that list in matrix coordinates, use column operations that preserve the integral lattice and keep track of the diagonal blocks that enter covariance. A basis adapted to a lattice flag gives an abstract proof of the factorization. The production proof of `UnitInd.exists_iwasawa` uses valuation elimination, which we follow here.

**Example (two one-dimensional blocks over $\mathbb Q_3$).** Let $P=B$ be upper triangular, $K=\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z_3)$, and $E_{ij}$ the matrix unit in row $i$, column $j$. Start with $x=\begin{pmatrix}0&3\\1&3\end{pmatrix}$. The last row has valuations $0,1$. Right multiplication by $U=1+E_{12}$ adds column 1 to column 2, giving last row $(1,4)$. The pivot $4$ is a unit. With $T=1-E_{21}/4$, column 1 becomes column 1 minus one quarter of column 2. Thus $b=xUT=\begin{pmatrix}-3/4&3\\0&4\end{pmatrix}$ and
$k=T^{-1}U^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&-1\\1/4&3/4\end{pmatrix}$.
Both operations and their inverses are integral. Multiplication gives $bk=x$ and $\det k=1$. The Levi is $m=\operatorname{diag}(-3/4,4)$ and $b=nm$ with $n=1+(3/4)E_{12}$. The radical coordinate is multiplied by $(-3/4)/4=-3/16$ under conjugation by $m$. Hence $\delta_B(b)=1/3$. For trivial Levi coefficients, the right $K$-fixed section with value $1$ at the identity has value $1/\sqrt 3$ at $x$, using the positive square root. The inverse half-power at this same Levi element, used by the normalized Jacquet twist, is $\sqrt 3$.

**Example (a $(2,1)$ parabolic over $\mathbb Q_3$).** Take
$x=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&9\\0&1&0\\1&3&3\end{pmatrix}$,
with determinant $-9$. In the last row the valuations are $0,1,1$. Multiply by $U=1+E_{13}$ to obtain $(1,3,4)$. Multiply next by $T=1-E_{31}/4-3E_{32}/4$, clearing both entries to the left of $4$. The complementary block already satisfies the required block conditions, and its second row $(0,1)$ would also require no further elimination. The resulting factors are
$b=\begin{pmatrix}-9/4&-27/4&9\\0&1&0\\0&0&4\end{pmatrix}$ and
$k=T^{-1}U^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&-1\\0&1&0\\1/4&3/4&3/4\end{pmatrix}$.
The inverse of $k$ is $UT$, with integral entries, and multiplying $b$ by $k$ restores each row of $x$. The first diagonal block is $A=\begin{pmatrix}-9/4&-27/4\\0&1\end{pmatrix}$ and the second is $d=4$. The unipotent factor in $b=nm$ is $n=1+(9/4)E_{13}$. Conjugation on the two-entry column of the radical is $Y\mapsto AY/4$, of determinant $\det(A)/4^2=-9/64$. Therefore $\delta_P(b)=1/9$. The normalized spherical section for trivial coefficients has value $1/3$ at $x$, and the Jacquet twist at $m$ is $3$.

**Definitions.** Let $F$ be a nonarchimedean local field with discrete valuation $v$, ring of integers $\mathcal O$, uniformizer $\varpi$, and residue cardinality $q$. Normalize $|t|_F=q^{-v(t)}$ for $t\ne0$. Let $I$ be a finite coordinate set and $\ell:I\to A$ a map to a finite linearly ordered set. Write $I_a=\ell^{-1}(a)$, $d_a=|I_a|$, $G=\mathrm{GL}_I(F)$ and $K=\mathrm{GL}_I(\mathcal O)$. The block upper subgroup $P$ consists of matrices with $x_{ij}=0$ if $\ell(i)>\ell(j)$. Its diagonal blocks define $m:P\to M=\prod_a\mathrm{GL}_{I_a}(F)$, and $N$ is the kernel of $m$. Write $L(m)$ for the block diagonal embedding. Empty blocks have determinant $1$.

**Lemma (integral elimination).** Every invertible matrix over a valued field can be made block upper triangular for the specified labels by right multiplication by an integral invertible matrix.

**Theorem (local Iwasawa and its compact model).** For each such choice of $F,I,\ell$ and every $x\in G$, there exist $n\in N$, $m_0\in M$ and $k\in K$ with $x=nL(m_0)k$. The subgroup $K$ is compact open, $P$ is closed, and $m$ is continuous. The space $P\setminus G$ of left $P$-orbits is compact Hausdorff and is homeomorphic to $(P\cap K)\setminus K$. The conjugation modulus on $N$ is the locally constant character
$\delta_P(p)=\prod_a|\det m(p)_a|_F^{\sum_{b>a}d_b-\sum_{b<a}d_b}$.
It equals $1$ on $P\cap K$. For every smooth complex $M$-representation $\sigma$, restriction identifies normalized induction with the right-smooth functions $h:K\to V_\sigma$ satisfying $h(tk)=\sigma(m(t))h(k)$ for $t\in P\cap K$. All the structural assertions hold factorwise for finite products.

**Proof of the lemma and factorization.** Right multiplication by $1+S$ with integral $S$ and $S^2=0$ lies in $K$ because its inverse is $1-S$. Induct on $|I|$, starting with the unique empty matrix. Choose $i_0$ of maximal block label. Row $i_0$ is nonzero by invertibility. Choose an entry of largest absolute value in that row, say in column $j_0$. If the diagonal entry already has that value in absolute value, take $U=1$. Otherwise take $U=1+E_{j_0 i_0}$. The ultrametric equality for a sum of unequal absolute values gives a nonzero pivot $c=(xU)_{i_0 i_0}$ of largest absolute value. This describes the archive's maximal multiplicative valuation in absolute-value notation.

Put $S=-\sum_{j\ne i_0}(xU)_{i_0 j}E_{i_0 j}/c$. The coefficients are integral by maximality of $|c|_F$, and $S^2=0$ since all the summands have row $i_0$ and different column from $i_0$. With $T=1+S$, the row $i_0$ of $xUT$ is zero except for its entry $c$. Expansion along that row gives $\det(xUT)=c\det A_0$ for the submatrix $A_0$ on the complementary indices. In particular $A_0$ is invertible. Equivalently the corresponding complementary submatrix of $(xUT)^{-1}$ is its inverse, as in the Lean proof.

By induction $A_0=b_0k_0$ for the restricted block labels. Extend $k_0^{-1}$ on the complement by the identity at $i_0$, with zero cross terms, to obtain $V\in K$. In $b=xUTV$, the complementary submatrix is $b_0$ and row $i_0$ still has only its diagonal entry. A forbidden entry cannot have column $i_0$, by maximality of $\ell(i_0)$. Every other forbidden entry vanishes in the cleared row or in $b_0$. Hence $b\in P$ and $x=bV^{-1}T^{-1}U^{-1}$. The subgroups and their inverse conditions arise from actual changes of integral basis of $\mathcal O^I$.

For an invertible block upper matrix the product of diagonal-block determinants is nonzero, by the determinant formula for block triangular matrices. Diagonal extraction respects multiplication and inverses. If $m_0=m(b)$, then $u=L(m_0)^{-1}b$ has identity diagonal blocks and belongs to $N$. Moving $L(m_0)$ to the right gives $n=L(m_0)uL(m_0)^{-1}$. In the archive the bijection $e:(\coprod_a I_a)\simeq I$ supplies $\ell$, and the order-equivalence of the block labels with their ranks supplies the natural-number labels used by elimination. This yields the order $N M K$ used at the production call site. [Lean, read]

**Proof of the topological assertions.** Invertible matrices and their inverses depend continuously on their entries, by the adjugate and nonzero determinant formula. Thus the topology of the group of units agrees with the topology of its image in the matrix space. In the archive $K$ is the image of the units over $\mathcal O$. It consists exactly of the matrices for which both $g$ and $g^{-1}$ have entries in $\mathcal O$: the two integral matrices then multiply in either order to the identity over $\mathcal O$. Since $\mathcal O$ is open, these two finite sets of conditions define an open subgroup. The graph $g\mapsto (g,g^{-1})$ identifies $K$ with the closed subset of the compact product $\operatorname{Mat}_I(\mathcal O)^2$ cut out by the two inverse equations. This is the compactness argument of `SplitFlip.isCompact_KO`. The block-zero equations are closed in $G$. The identity diagonal conditions further cut out the closed subgroup $N$. The entries of $m(p)$ and its inverse are extracted from $p$ and $p^{-1}$, so $m$ is continuous. [Lean, read]

For the quotient, the map $G\to P\setminus G$ is open since saturation of an open set under $P$ is open. If $Pg_1\ne Pg_2$, then $g_2g_1^{-1}\notin P$. Closedness and continuity give neighborhoods of $g_1,g_2$ whose pairwise products in this order avoid $P$. Their images separate the two cosets. Restricting the quotient map to $K$ gives a continuous surjection by the factorization, and the exact fibers are left cosets of $P\cap K$. The resulting continuous bijection from a compact space to a Hausdorff space is a homeomorphism. If $J\le K$ is open, the open cosets of $J$ cover $K$ and compactness makes $K/J$ finite. Therefore $P\setminus G/J$ has a finite set of representatives in $K$. If $J$ is normal in $K$, their stabilizers for left $P$-covariance are all $P\cap J$, a closed subgroup of $K$. Principal congruence kernels give a neighborhood basis of such $J$ because the entrywise congruence conditions form a basis. These consequences identify precisely where compactness and openness enter.

**Proof of the modulus and restriction assertions.** Use one additive Haar measure in each upper off-diagonal coordinate of $N$. In those coordinates a translation by a fixed radical element acts by successive translations: an entry in block $(a,b)$ changes by a constant and terms involving entries of smaller distance between block labels. Fubini in decreasing distance shows that the product measure is translation invariant. Conjugation by $L(m_0)$ acts on block $(a,b)$ by $Y\mapsto (m_0)_aY(m_0)_b^{-1}$. The two operations have determinants $(\det(m_0)_a)^{d_b}$ and $(\det(m_0)_b)^{-d_a}$ by acting on columns and rows. Linear changes scale Haar volumes by the absolute determinant, which follows by elementary elimination into coordinate dilations, permutations and shears. In one coordinate $\varpi\mathcal O$ has index $q$ in $\mathcal O$, and a unit preserves $\mathcal O$, giving exactly the normalization $|\cdot|_F$. Thus the displayed formula gives $\operatorname{vol}(pEp^{-1})=\delta_P(p)\operatorname{vol}(E)$ on the radical. Conjugation by an element of $N$ has factor one by left and right translation invariance. This is the convention in which induction uses the positive half-power and the normalized Jacquet module uses the inverse half-power. The determinant formula is multiplicative and locally constant, since absolute value is constant on each nonzero valuation shell. For $p\in P\cap K$, its diagonal blocks and their inverses are integral, so each block determinant is a unit. In fact $nL(m_0)\in K$ precisely when $n\in K$ and all $(m_0)_a$ are integral units. For the forward implication extract both diagonal parts as above and divide by the resulting $L(m_0)\in K$. The reverse implication is closure under multiplication.

For the compact model, induction has covariance with $\tau(p)=\delta_P(p)^{1/2}\sigma(m(p))$ and right action $R_g f(x)=f(xg)$. Since $\delta_P=1$ on $P\cap K$, restriction has the stated covariance. Conversely define $f(pk)=\tau(p)h(k)$. If $pk=p'k'$, then $t=p'^{-1}p=k'k^{-1}\in P\cap K$ and $h(k')=\tau(t)h(k)$, proving independence. An open right stabilizer of $h$ in $K$ is open in $G$ and fixes $f$. Thus these maps are inverse. The support of a smooth function is its nonzero locus, which is closed because its zero locus is open. Covariance makes it left $P$-saturated, so its image is closed in the compact quotient. For a finite product, take products of the factors, compact subgroups and parabolics. Conjugation on the product radical multiplies moduli and their positive square roots. A missing factor or a zero-dimensional block introduces only a trivial group.

**Which hypotheses travel to a split unitary group?** The algebraic elimination uses only a field with a valuation ring: the largest of finitely many multiplicative valuations exists, the chosen ratios are integral, and the unequal-valuation sum rule supplies the pivot. The compact-open argument and the normalization by $q$ use the local field hypotheses. For transport, let the quadratic algebra at the place be $F\times F$ with the swapping involution. A nondegenerate Hermitian space then consists of two summands in perfect duality; in dual bases an isometry is $(g,(g^{-1})^{\mathsf T})$. Indeed preservation of the pairing forces the second matrix to be inverse transpose, and every first matrix gives such an isometry. Projection and inverse transpose are continuous in these coordinates. The parabolic must correspond to a flag in the first space, together with the dual constraints in the second. For the chosen compact subgroup to transport to $\mathrm{GL}_I(\mathcal O)$ in the same coordinates, take the first integral lattice spanned by a basis adapted to the flag and the second to be its dual. Then preserving both is equivalent to $g,g^{-1}$ being integral. The transported conjugation modulus agrees because a group isomorphism transports Haar measure on the radical and its scaling factor. A different frame transports the compact subgroup as well as the parabolic. These conditions are the split construction used to interpret matrix models. An involution on a quadratic field extension gives a different unitary group and the projection argument does not apply.

The archive consumers below work directly with $G_i=\mathrm{GL}_{m_i}(F)$, $P_i=\operatorname{parab}(F,f_i)$ and $K_i=\mathrm{KO}(F,m_i)$ for each block bijection $f_i:\coprod_j c_{ij}\simeq m_i$. The ambient relative group is the product $\operatorname{Levi}(F,m)=\prod_iG_i$. Accordingly these calls require a nonarchimedean local field and the finite ordered block data. No separate hypothesis about a unitary frame enters them. The split unitary argument above explains a possible transport to this matrix setting.

**Where normalized induction uses the supply.**

| Archive consumer | Input from this subsection | Outcome at the call |
|---|---|---|
| `levi_integral_facts` | $G=PK$, closed $P,N$, continuous $m$, and modulus trivial on integral Levi blocks | Passes the structural hypotheses to the relative functors. |
| `relInd_piTensor` | Each $G_i=P_iK_i$, $K_i$ compact and $P_i$ closed, continuous projection and smooth half-power | Uses the open normal levels and inherited finite bump expansion to prove surjectivity of product evaluation. The covariance identity uses the product half-power. |
| `relInd_surjective` | Product $G=PK$, closed $P$, compact $K$, continuous product projection and smooth half-power | Supplies these to `smInd_coinvariants`. The inherited lift of a fixed coefficient value uses compact $P\cap J$. |
| `parInd`, `relInd` and the corresponding Jacquet functors | Positive half-power after Levi projection for covariance; inverse half-power on coinvariants | Fixes the conventions of [§7.4.3.1](#7431-spherical-transfer-at-a-split-place) and the cell multiplicity formula of [§7.4.3.6](#7436-composition-factors-of-a-cell-module). |

**Boundary and sources.** In $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_3)$, the open noncompact subgroup $K'=\mathbb Q_3^\times K$ (scalars times $K$) still satisfies $G=BK'$. But $K'/K$ has infinitely many cosets represented by $3^j 1$ for $j\in\mathbb Z$. Indeed a scalar matrix belongs to $K$ exactly when the scalar has valuation zero. Also $B\cap K'$ is noncompact: the continuous determinant valuation has unbounded image there. Thus the two compactness deductions for a proposed compact tail, finite index of an open level and compactness of the parabolic intersection, can both fail. Here the original $B\setminus G$ is still compact because the correct $K$ exists. A noncompact substitute can even destroy the factorization: taking the diagonal torus for the right subgroup gives only $B$, and the matrix $\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}$ lies outside it.

The compact restriction model and the positive half-power appear in Casselman's §3.1. Bernstein and Zelevinsky give the covariant-function and localization conventions together. The elimination and integral-subgroup arguments above follow the Alpöge–Claude archive at the pinned ranges in the source ledger. The compact quotient, split transport and hand calculations are textbook derivations. The [exterior-tensor maps](#7438-jacquet-modules-and-induction-of-exterior-tensors), [open normal levels](#7439-open-normal-levels-in-compact-groups), compact averages and bumps are used as established above. The separate GroundTheory block-Iwasawa argument and the Mathlib valuation-topology leaves retain their interface status in this subsection.
