#### 7.4.3.11 The compact integral and unitarisability of normalized induction

The compact model evaluates sections on $K=\mathrm{GL}_n(\mathcal O)$. A right translate evaluates a section on $Kg$, which can leave $K$. The square of the normalization factor compensates for the change of volume when the translated argument is brought back to $K$.

**Two computations over $\mathbb Q_3$.** For blocks $(r,1)$ let $g=\operatorname{diag}(3I_r,1)$ and $k(x)=\begin{pmatrix}I_r&0\\x&1\end{pmatrix}$ for a row $x$. The parabolic fixes the projective class of the last row under left multiplication. On the chart in which its last coordinate is nonzero, divide by that coordinate to obtain $(x,1)$. Take the trivial Levi representation on $\mathbb C$. On $K$, take the indicator of the compact open chart subset $x\in3\mathbb Z_3^r$, with value zero elsewhere. Extend to a normalized section by $G=PK$ and the covariance defined below. The extension is well-defined because the modulus on $P\cap K$ is one. The restriction is fixed by the first principal congruence subgroup, as its support is a condition on reduction modulo $3$, so the extension is smooth. Its support modulo $P$ is compact.

With $\mu(K)=1$, reduction gives uniform probability on $\mathrm{GL}_{r+1}(\mathbb F_3)$ by surjectivity and equal coset masses. Right multiplication acts transitively on nonzero last rows, so each row has equal probability. For a bottom row $(a,b)$ of $k\in K$, the initial support means $b$ is a unit and $a$ reduces to zero. After translation the last row is $(3a,b)$, which lies in the chart support precisely when $b\ne0$ and $a/b$ is integral. Primitivity of $(a,b)$ makes this equivalent to $b$ being a unit. On this set write $k=p\,k(a/b)$ with $p=k\,k(a/b)^{-1}\in P\cap K$. The identity $k(x)g=gk(3x)$ gives the translated amplitude $\delta_P(g)^{1/2}$.

- For $\mathrm{GL}_2$ the initial and translated support masses are $2/8=1/4$ and $6/8=3/4$. The one-dimensional conjugation multiplies the coordinate by $3$, so the amplitude is $1/\sqrt{3}$ and the translated squared norm is $(1/3)(3/4)=1/4$. With unnormalized covariance the translated squared norm is $3/4$. For a radical test with additive Haar mass one on $\mathbb Z_3$, right multiplication by $1+(1/3)E_{12}$ changes $x$ to $x+1/3$. The translate of the indicator of $\mathbb Z_3$ has support $-1/3+\mathbb Z_3$ and still has integral one.
- For $\mathrm{GL}_3$ with blocks $(2,1)$ the support masses are $2/26=1/13$ and $18/26=9/13$. Both radical coordinates scale by $3$ under conjugation, so the translated amplitude is $1/3$ and the squared norm is $(1/9)(9/13)=1/13$. With unnormalized covariance the squared norm is $9/13$. Write $u(x,y)=1+xE_{13}+yE_{23}$. Right multiplication by $u(1/3,2)$ sends $(x,y)$ to $(x+1/3,y+2)$. The indicator of $3\mathbb Z_3\times(1+\mathbb Z_3)$ translates to the indicator of $(-1/3+3\mathbb Z_3)\times(-1+\mathbb Z_3)$. Each has integral $1/3$ with $\mathbb Z_3^2$ of mass one.

**Definitions and standing inputs.** Let $F$ be any nonarchimedean local field with ring of integers $\mathcal O$ and absolute value normalized by the residue cardinality. Fix any finite ordered set of blocks and finite coordinate sets $I_a$ with a bijection from their disjoint union to the matrix index set. Let $G$ be the general linear group, $P$ the upper block parabolic, $N$ its unipotent radical, $M$ the block diagonal Levi, $m:P\to M$ its projection, and $K$ the integral general linear group. Use Borel structures throughout, and write $\delta(s)$ for the conjugation modulus on $N$ of $s\in M$, with $\delta_P=\delta\circ m$. From [§7.4.3.10](#74310-local-iwasawa-decomposition-for-normalized-induction) we use the local Iwasawa supplies $G=NMK$, compactness and openness of $K$, and $vs\in K$ if and only if $v\in N_0=N\cap K$ and $s\in M_0=M\cap K$ for $v\in N,\ s\in M$. We use the inherited algebraic formula for the positive locally constant character $\delta$. Empty products have determinant and mass one. [Lean]

**Lemma (coordinate Haar measure).** For every additive Haar measure $\lambda$ on $F$, transport the product of its copies on $J=\prod_{a<b}\operatorname{Mat}_{I_a,I_b}(F)$ by $X\mapsto 1+X$. The resulting measure $\nu$ on $N$ is Haar and right invariant. For Borel $E\subset N$ and $s\in M$, it satisfies $\nu(sEs^{-1})=\delta(s)\nu(E)$.

**Proof.** The coordinate map is a homeomorphism by entry extraction and the finite nilpotent inverse polynomial. Right multiplication by $1+U$ induces $X\mapsto U+X+XU$. For output block $(a,b)$ the additional linear term is $\sum_{a<c<b}X_{ac}U_{cb}$. Ordering the coordinates by column-block label gives a triangular matrix with identity diagonal blocks and determinant one. For left multiplication the extra term $UX$ depends on later row blocks, so the row-block ordering gives determinant one as well. Conjugation induces $X\mapsto sXs^{-1}$, whose absolute determinant gives the inherited character formula.

All these changes are Borel. For an invertible linear map $T$ of a finite coordinate space, the image of a Borel set has measure scaled by $|\det T|_F$. For diagonal scaling this follows on rectangles from one-dimensional Haar scaling. For a shear, hold the unchanged coordinates fixed and use translation invariance on the changed coordinate, then Tonelli. Permuting coordinates preserves the product of equal measures. Elimination into these operations proves the assertion for $T$. Additive translation also preserves the product. Thus both affine group translations preserve $\nu$. The product measure is finite on compacts and positive on nonempty open sets, and the homeomorphism transfers these properties. Conjugation scales images by $\delta(s)$, so its pushforward has the inverse factor. Haar uniqueness transfers the same image identity to any Haar measure on $N$, with the same scalar on both sides. [Lean, read]

**Proposition (right translation of a covariant integral).** For every Haar measure $\mu$ on $G$, Borel function $\phi:G\to[0,\infty]$ satisfying $\phi(px)=\delta_P(p)\phi(x)$ for every $p\in P$ and $x\in G$, and $g\in G$, one has $\int_K\phi(xg)\,d\mu(x)=\int_K\phi(x)\,d\mu(x)$ as an extended nonnegative integral.

**Proof.** Haar on $G$ is two-sided. Its positive modular character is trivial on central elements since their right and left translations agree. The scalar equality is evaluated on $K$ and cancelled using $0<\mu(K)<\infty$. A positive character is constant on conjugacy classes. Permute an invertible diagonal matrix in every possible way and multiply the results. The product is central because each diagonal entry occurs equally often in each position. Thus the character value on the original diagonal matrix to the power given by the number of permutations is one. Each transvection is conjugate to its inverse by a diagonal sign change, so its positive character value has square one, including in characteristic two. Diagonal matrices and transvections generate by elimination. The trivial group is immediate. The same argument applies to each Levi factor.

Let $\tau$ be the product of Haar measures on the Levi factors and put $j(s,v)=vs$ on $M\times N$. Set $c=\nu(N_0)\tau(M_0)$. These subgroups are compact and open: $N$ is closed by the block equations, and $M_0$ is a finite product of integral general linear groups. Both contain the identity, hence $0<c<\infty$. All groups involved are second-countable, locally compact and Hausdorff, so Haar and the finite product measures are sigma-finite. Multiplication, inversion, $j$ and the positive real character $\delta$ are continuous. The indicators, their compositions and the products with $\phi$ below are jointly measurable. Extended nonnegative products use $0\cdot\infty=0$.

Fix any $x=utw$ with $u\in N,\ t\in M,\ w\in K$. The condition $j(s,v)x\in K$ is equivalent to $v(sus^{-1})\in N_0$ and $st\in M_0$. For each $s$ the first condition has $\nu$-mass $\nu(N_0)$ by right invariance. The second has $\tau$-mass $\tau(M_0)$ by right invariance on $M$. Thus the integral of $1_K(j(s,v)x)$ over $M\times N$ is $c$ for every $x$.

For the inverse condition use $j(s,v)^{-1}x=(s^{-1}v^{-1}us)(s^{-1}t)w$. Inverting within the two compact subgroups shows that membership in $K$ is equivalent to $u^{-1}v\in sN_0s^{-1}$ and $t^{-1}s\in M_0$. The first condition has $\nu$-mass $\delta(s)\nu(N_0)$ by left invariance and conjugation scaling. Multiplication by $\delta(s^{-1})$ gives $\nu(N_0)$. The second condition has mass $\tau(M_0)$ by left invariance. Therefore the integral of $1_K(j(s,v)^{-1}x)\delta(s^{-1})$ over $M\times N$ is also $c$ for every $x$.

Let $L=Kg=\{x:xg^{-1}\in K\}$. The first identity applied to $xg^{-1}$ gives the same value $c$ for the integral of $1_L(j(s,v)x)$. Now integrate $1_K(x)\phi(x)1_L(j(s,v)x)$ on $G\times(M\times N)$ with $\mu$ and $\tau\times\nu$. Integrating first in $(s,v)$ gives $c\int_K\phi\,d\mu$. By Tonelli we can first integrate in $x$. For fixed $(s,v)$ substitute $x=j(s,v)^{-1}z$ by left Haar invariance. Covariance inserts $\delta(s^{-1})$, giving the integrand $1_L(z)\phi(z)1_K(j(s,v)^{-1}z)\delta(s^{-1})$. The second fiber identity now gives $c\int_L\phi\,d\mu$. Cancel by the positive finite inverse of $c$, which works also for zero and infinite integral values. With $c=0$ all products would be zero, and with $c=\infty$ all positive integral values would give the same product. Finally use right Haar invariance to write $\int_L\phi\,d\mu=\int_K\phi(xg)\,d\mu(x)$. Both integrals use the same $\mu$, and a positive finite rescaling multiplies both by the same scalar. [Lean, read]

**Theorem (induced inner product).** For any complex representation $\rho$ of $M$ with invariant positive definite Hermitian form $B$ on $W$, set $I(\rho)$ to be the smooth space of functions $f:G\to W$ satisfying $f(px)=\delta_P(p)^{1/2}\rho(m(p))f(x)$ for every $p,x$. Here smoothness means an open stabilizer for the right action $(R_g f)(x)=f(xg)$. With $B$ linear in the first argument, the form $C(f,h)=\int_K B(f(x),h(x))\,d\mu(x)$ is invariant and positive definite.

**Proof.** Each section is constant on the neighborhoods $xU$ for its open stabilizer $U$. The scalar coefficients are locally constant and thus Borel and continuous, independently of any topology on $W$. They are bounded on $K$ and integrable since $\mu(K)<\infty$. This also holds for right-translated sections, which are smooth. Integral linearity and conjugation prove sesquilinearity and symmetry. For $f\ne0$, take a point $x=utw$ where $f(x)\ne0$. Covariance implies $f(w)\ne0$. On an open subset of $K$ around $w$ the diagonal coefficient is a strictly positive constant, and this set has positive Haar mass. Thus $C(f,f)>0$.

The function $\phi_f$ obtained by viewing $B(f(x),f(x))$ as a nonnegative real and then an extended nonnegative real satisfies the scalar law in the proposition. The two positive square roots multiply to $\delta_P(p)$ and $\rho(m(p))$ preserves $B$. This scalar law supplies $\delta(s^{-1})$ in the fiber average, which cancels the volume factor $\delta(s)$. The proposition gives equality of diagonal coefficients of the forms after integration. Both integrals are finite before conversion to real values. Set $D(f,h)=C(R_g f,R_g h)-C(f,h)$. Expanding the zero diagonal at $f+h$ and $f+ih$ gives $D(f,h)+D(h,f)=0$ and $-iD(f,h)+iD(h,f)=0$. Therefore $D(f,h)=0$. [Lean, read]

**Notes.** The unnormalized sections above show strict failure of the same compact norm even with trivial unitary inducing data. Compactness alone gives no integrability for a general Borel function: $x\mapsto |x|_3^{-1}$ on $\mathbb Z_3\setminus\{0\}$, valued zero at zero, has integral $\sum_{j\ge0}3^j(3^{-j}-3^{-j-1})=\infty$. The extended nonnegative proposition allows infinite values. The complex pairing uses finite integrability before positive-minus-negative or real-part conversion.

The archive tensors invariant positive forms on the Levi factors using [§7.4.3.12](#74312-tensor-positive-forms-on-algebraic-levi-coefficients) and applies the theorem to its two-block and finite-block products. [§7.4.3.13](#74313-invariant-complements-of-admissible-smooth-representations) follows the induced form into the splitting arguments. This supplies the unitarisability input in product, complement and cosocle arguments with their own hypotheses. A parallel real and complex extension of the scalar integral supplies the invariant contragredient pairing and a fixed-line construction. The conclusion is smooth unitarisability in the sense of an invariant positive form.

Casselman’s draft, §3.1, Propositions 3.1.1–3.1.4, pp. 32–33, gives the classical compact model, pairing and preservation of unitarity. His locally compact nonarchimedean field is $F$, his connected reductive group here is general linear, and his good compact subgroup is $K$ with $G=PK$. His determinant on the radical Lie algebra defines $\delta_P$. The two positive half-powers in his pairing give $\delta_P$, and his unitarity proposition uses the invariant positive pairing on the Levi. The extended nonnegative comparison of the two fiber masses above follows the archive. [Casselman’s draft](https://personal.math.ubc.ca/~cass/research/pdf/p-adic-book.pdf)

Local R3, with same-source preliminary review of the selected measure and induction claims. [Source and declaration ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-74311--compact-integration-and-normalized-induction).

#### 7.4.3.12 Tensor positive forms on algebraic Levi coefficients

In §7.4.3.11, normalized parabolic induction $I_P^G(\sigma)$ from a standard block-upper parabolic $P = MN \le G = \mathrm{GL}_n(F)$ inherited a $G$-invariant positive-definite Hermitian form
$$
C_{\mathrm{ind}}(f, h) = \int_K B_M\bigl(f(x), h(x)\bigr)\,d\mu(x)
$$
on the compact model $K = \mathrm{GL}_n(\mathcal O)$ as soon as the inducing representation $(\sigma, W)$ of the Levi subgroup $M \cong \prod_{a \in \alpha} \mathrm{GL}_{m_a}(F)$ carried an $M$-invariant positive-definite Hermitian form $B_M$ (`UnitInd.isUnitarisable_parInd`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:428–480`). Downstream in node `P17` and the local unitary-dual engine, however, parabolic induction is applied to **two-block products** $\pi_1 \times \pi_2 = \operatorname{prod}_2(\pi_1, \pi_2)$ and **finite-block products** $\operatorname{multiProd}_e((\pi_a)_{a \in \alpha})$, whose Levi representation is an **algebraic exterior tensor product** $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ or $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ of smooth factor representations $\pi_a$ (`IsSubquotientDetRepParInd.lean:321–323, 367–368`). Factor spaces $X_a$ can be infinite-dimensional over $\mathbb C$. The construction uses them as algebraic vector spaces.

Constructing $B_M$ on $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ and $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ in arbitrary dimension requires resolving two distinct algebraic issues before `isUnitarisable_parInd` can be called:

1. **Sesquilinear slot conventions and descent across tensor relations.** On pure tensors, one wants $B_\otimes(v_1 \otimes w_1, v_2 \otimes w_2) = B_1(v_1, v_2) B_2(w_1, w_2)$ and $C\bigl(\bigotimes_a x_a, \bigotimes_a y_a\bigr) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(x_a, y_a)$. Why does this product formula descend modulo all $\mathbb C$-balancing relations $(\lambda v) \otimes w = v \otimes (\lambda w)$ in *both* tensor slots, and how does the archive reconcile its convention—$\mathbb C$-linear in the first slot and conjugate-linear in the second (`IsUnitarisable`, `IsUnitarisableOfCharIff.lean:305–308`)—with Mathlib's `InnerProductSpace.Core ℂ`, which is conjugate-linear in the first slot and $\mathbb C$-linear in the second (`UnitInd.core`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:16–30`)?
2. **Strict positive definiteness on entangled sums without infinite-dimensional bases or completion.** A general nonzero element $z = \sum_{k=1}^N r_k\, v_k \otimes w_k \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ is a finite sum of pure tensors whose factor vectors $\{v_k\}$ and $\{w_k\}$ need not be orthogonal; in the expanded sum $B_\otimes(z, z) = \sum_{k,\ell=1}^N r_k \overline{r_\ell}\, B_1(v_k, v_\ell) B_2(w_k, w_\ell)$, individual off-diagonal terms can have negative real parts or nonzero imaginary parts. Instead of choosing Hamel bases of infinite-dimensional $V$ and $W$ or completing to a Hilbert space, both Mathlib's `TensorProduct.lean` (`:98–130`) and the archive's `isUnitarisable_piTensor` (`IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:62–74, 118–144`) isolate a **finite-dimensional support subspace inside each factor for a single tensor $z$**, orthonormalize those finite-dimensional subspaces, and reduce $B_\otimes(z, z)$ to a finite Parseval sum of squared moduli.

---

**Worked Calculation 1 (Binary non-orthogonal Hermitian Gram matrices, an entangled rank-$2$ tensor, and $(G \times H)$-invariance).** Take $V = W = \mathbb C^2$ with standard basis $u_1 = (1, 0)^\top$, $u_2 = (0, 1)^\top$ of $V$ and $w_1 = (1, 0)^\top$, $w_2 = (0, 1)^\top$ of $W$ [Expository]. Under the archive's convention $B(x, y) = x^\top G \overline{y}$ ($\mathbb C$-linear in $x$, conjugate-linear in $y$), equip $V$ and $W$ with the non-orthogonal Hermitian Gram matrices
$$
G_1 = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 1+i \\ 1-i & 3 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad G_2 = \begin{pmatrix} 4 & 2-2i \\ 2+2i & 3 \end{pmatrix}.
$$
Both matrices equal their conjugate transposes ($G_a^\top = \overline{G_a}$) and have strictly positive leading principal minors: $(G_1)_{11} = 1 \gt 0$, $\det G_1 = 3 - \lvert 1+i \rvert^2 = 1 \gt 0$, and $(G_2)_{11} = 4 \gt 0$, $\det G_2 = 12 - \lvert 2-2i \rvert^2 = 4 \gt 0$, so $B_1$ and $B_2$ are strictly positive definite despite $B_1(u_1, u_2) = 1+i \ne 0$ and $B_2(w_1, w_2) = 2-2i \ne 0$.

1. *Orthonormalization (`exists_orthonormal` and the `UnitInd.core` flip).* In `UnitInd.core`, the flipped inner product is $\langle v, v' \rangle_{\mathrm{core}} := B_1(v', v)$, which takes the exact same values on orthonormal pairs ($\delta_{ij} = \delta_{ji}$). Applying Gram–Schmidt to $(u_1, u_2)$ under $B_1$ gives $e_1 = u_1 = (1, 0)^\top$ ($B_1(e_1, e_1) = 1$) and projection scalar $B_1(u_2, e_1) = 1-i$, hence
   $$
   e_2 = u_2 - (1-i)e_1 = \begin{pmatrix} -1+i \\ 1 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad B_1(e_2, e_2) = \lvert -1+i \rvert^2 \cdot 1 + (-1+i)(1+i) + (1-i)(-1-i) + 3 = 2 - 2 - 2 + 3 = 1,
   $$
   so $(e_1, e_2)$ is $B_1$-orthonormal, with inverse expansion $u_1 = e_1$, $u_2 = (1-i)e_1 + e_2$. On $W$, $B_2(w_1, w_1) = 4$ gives $f_1 = \tfrac{1}{2} w_1 = (\tfrac{1}{2}, 0)^\top$ and projection scalar $B_2(w_2, f_1) = \tfrac{1}{2}(2+2i) = 1+i$, hence
   $$
   f_2 = w_2 - (1+i)f_1 = \begin{pmatrix} -\tfrac{1+i}{2} \\ 1 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad B_2(f_2, f_2) = \tfrac{1}{2}\cdot 4 + \bigl(-\tfrac{1+i}{2}\bigr)(2-2i) + (2+2i)\bigl(-\tfrac{1-i}{2}\bigr) + 3 = 2 - 2 - 2 + 3 = 1,
   $$
   so $(f_1, f_2)$ is $B_2$-orthonormal, with inverse expansion $w_1 = 2 f_1$, $w_2 = (1+i)f_1 + f_2$.
2. *Evaluating $B_\otimes$ on an entangled tensor both directly and via Parseval.* Consider
   $$
   z = u_1 \otimes w_2 - i\, u_2 \otimes w_1 \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W.
   $$
   In the basis $\{u_p \otimes w_q\}_{p,q \in \{1,2\}}$, the coefficient matrix of $z$ is $\begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1 \\ -i & 0 \end{pmatrix}$ of determinant $i \ne 0$ (Schmidt rank $2$), so $z$ is **not** a pure tensor. Expanding $B_\otimes(z, z)$ directly from pure-tensor bilinearity yields four terms, two of which are strictly negative:
   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   B_\otimes(z, z)
   &= B_1(u_1, u_1) B_2(w_2, w_2) + 1\cdot \overline{(-i)}\, B_1(u_1, u_2) B_2(w_2, w_1) \\
   &\quad + (-i)\cdot 1\, B_1(u_2, u_1) B_2(w_1, w_2) + \lvert -i \rvert^2 B_1(u_2, u_2) B_2(w_1, w_1) \\
   &= 1 \cdot 3 + i(1+i)(2+2i) + (-i)(1-i)(2-2i) + 1 \cdot 3 \cdot 4 \\
   &= 3 + i(4i) + (-i)(-4i) + 12 = 3 - 4 - 4 + 12 = 7 \gt 0.
   \end{aligned}
   $$
   Rewriting $z$ in the orthonormal product basis $\{e_p \otimes f_q\}_{p,q \in \{1,2\}}$ makes positivity manifest without cancellation:
   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   z &= e_1 \otimes \bigl((1+i)f_1 + f_2\bigr) - i\bigl((1-i)e_1 + e_2\bigr) \otimes (2 f_1) \\
   &= (-1-i)\, e_1 \otimes f_1 + 1\, e_1 \otimes f_2 - 2i\, e_2 \otimes f_1 + 0\, e_2 \otimes f_2,
   \end{aligned}
   $$
   whose Parseval norm square is $\lvert -1-i \rvert^2 + \lvert 1 \rvert^2 + \lvert -2i \rvert^2 + 0 = 2 + 1 + 4 = 7 = B_\otimes(z, z)$. Pairing $z$ against $z_0 = u_1 \otimes w_1 + u_2 \otimes w_2$ gives the complex off-diagonal value
   $$
   B_\otimes(z, z_0) = 1(2+2i) + (1+i)(3) - i(1-i)(4) - i(3)(2-2i) = (2+2i) + (3+3i) - (4+4i) - (6+6i) = -5 - 5i,
   $$
   with $B_\otimes(z_0, z) = -5 + 5i = \overline{B_\otimes(z, z_0)}$.
3. *Explicit $(G \times H)$-invariance.* Let $G = H = \mathbb Z$ act on $V$ and $W$ via powers of
   $$
   M_1 = \begin{pmatrix} -1+i & 3i \\ 1 & 1-i \end{pmatrix}, \qquad M_2 = \frac{1}{5}\begin{pmatrix} -1-4i & -2-4i \\ 8 & 7+4i \end{pmatrix}.
   $$
   Direct matrix multiplication checks $M_1^\top G_1 \overline{M_1} = G_1$ ($\det M_1 = -i$) and $M_2^\top G_2 \overline{M_2} = G_2$ ($\det M_2 = \frac{3-4i}{5}$). Under $M_1 \otimes M_2$, $z$ maps to $\frac{1}{5}\bigl((-1+i, 1)^\top \otimes (-2-4i, 7+4i)^\top - i(3i, 1-i)^\top \otimes (-1-4i, 8)^\top\bigr)$, and expanding the four rotated Gram pairings reproduces $B_\otimes\bigl((M_1 \otimes M_2)z, (M_1 \otimes M_2)z\bigr) = 7$ and $B_\otimes\bigl((M_1 \otimes M_2)z, (M_1 \otimes M_2)z_0\bigr) = -5 - 5i$.

---

**Worked Calculation 2 (Three-factor infinite-dimensional smooth Levi coefficient over $\mathbb Q_3$, single-tensor finite-support subspace, `tensorCons`, and translation out of the subspace).** Let $F = \mathbb Q_3$, $\mathcal O = \mathbb Z_3$, and consider the three-block $(2, 2, 1)$ Levi $M = \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_3) \times \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_3) \times \mathrm{GL}_1(\mathbb Q_3)$ of $\mathrm{GL}_5(\mathbb Q_3)$ indexed by $\alpha = \mathrm{Fin}\,3 = \{0, 1, 2\}$ [Expository]:

- For $a \in \{0, 1\}$, let $(X_a, \pi_a) = I_{B_2}^{\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_3)}(\mathbf{1})$ be the smooth normalized unramified principal series of $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_3)$, equipped with the $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_3)$-invariant positive-definite Hermitian form $B_a(\phi, \psi) = \int_{K_2} \phi(k)\overline{\psi(k)}\,d\mu_2(k)$ ($K_2 = \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z_3)$, $\mu_2(K_2) = 1$) inherited from §7.4.3.11. Because smooth sections may vary on arbitrarily deep principal congruence subgroups $K_2(3^m) = I_2 + 3^m \operatorname{Mat}_2(\mathbb Z_3)$ ($m \ge 1$), **both $X_0$ and $X_1$ are infinite-dimensional over $\mathbb C$**.
- For $a = 2$, let $(X_2, \pi_2) = \operatorname{ofChar}(\chi)$ on $\mathbb C$, where $\chi : \mathbb Q_3^\times \to \mathbb C^\times$ is the unramified unitary character $\chi(3^m u) = i^m$ ($u \in \mathbb Z_3^\times$, $\chi(3) = i$), with invariant positive form $B_2(z, w) = z\overline{w}$ (`isUnitarisable_ofChar_iff`, `IsUnitarisableOfCharIff.lean:311–332`).

On $K_2 = \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z_3)$, reduction modulo $3$ maps $K_2$ surjectively onto $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb F_3)$ (order $48$) with equal Haar coset masses, and the left Borel image $B_2(\mathbb F_3)$ has order $12$. Hence $K_2$ partitions into four clopen tubes $(C_\infty, C_0, C_1, C_2)$ indexed by the projective class $[c : d] \in \mathbb P^1(\mathbb F_3)$ of the bottom row $(c, d) \bmod 3$ ($[1:0], [0:1], [1:1], [2:1]$), each of exact Haar measure $\mu_2(C_s) = \tfrac{12}{48} = \tfrac{1}{4}$. On sections constant on each $C_s$, written as $4$-tuples $(\xi_\infty, \xi_0, \xi_1, \xi_2) \in \mathbb C^4 \subset X_a$, the compact integral restricts to $B_a(\xi, \eta) = \tfrac{1}{4}\sum_{s} \xi_s \overline{\eta_s}$.

Now fix inside the infinite-dimensional algebraic tensor product $\bigotimes_{a \in \mathrm{Fin}\,3} X_a$ the single non-simple tensor
$$
v = \phi_1 \otimes \psi_2 \otimes 1 - (1-i)\, \phi_2 \otimes \psi_1 \otimes i,
$$
where $\phi_1 = (2, 0, 0, 0)$, $\phi_2 = (2+2i, 2, 0, 0) \in X_0$ and $\psi_1 = (1, 1, 1, 1)$, $\psi_2 = (3, 3, 1, 1) \in X_1$.

1. *Finite-support subspaces and `exists_orthonormal` (`IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:118–136`).* Although $\dim_{\mathbb C} X_0 = \dim_{\mathbb C} X_1 = \infty$, the single presentation of $v$ involves only $S_0 = \{\phi_1, \phi_2\} \subset X_0$, $S_1 = \{\psi_1, \psi_2\} \subset X_1$, and $S_2 = \{1, i\} \subset X_2$, whose spans $U_0 = \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}(S_0)$, $U_1 = \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}(S_1)$, and $U_2 = \mathbb C$ have finite dimensions $(d_0, d_1, d_2) = (2, 2, 1)$. Both spanning pairs are non-orthogonal:
   $$
   G^{(0)} = \bigl(B_0(\phi_p, \phi_q)\bigr) = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 1-i \\ 1+i & 3 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad G^{(1)} = \bigl(B_1(\psi_p, \psi_q)\bigr) = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 2 \\ 2 & 5 \end{pmatrix}.
   $$
   Orthonormalizing $U_0$, $U_1$, and $U_2$ yields
   $$
   b_{0,1} = (2, 0, 0, 0),\quad b_{0,2} = \phi_2 - (1+i)b_{0,1} = (0, 2, 0, 0), \qquad
   b_{1,1} = (1, 1, 1, 1),\quad b_{1,2} = \psi_2 - 2 b_{1,1} = (1, 1, -1, -1),
   $$
   and $b_{2,1} = 1$, satisfying $B_a(b_{a,p}, b_{a,q}) = \delta_{pq}$.
2. *Agreement of Parseval, `exists_piForm`, and head–tail `tensorCons`.* Substituting $\phi_1 = b_{0,1}$, $\phi_2 = (1+i)b_{0,1} + b_{0,2}$, $\psi_1 = b_{1,1}$, $\psi_2 = 2 b_{1,1} + b_{1,2}$, and $-(1-i)\, i = -1-i$ expands $v$ in the four orthonormal product tensors $E_{p,q,1} = b_{0,p} \otimes b_{1,q} \otimes 1$:
   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   v &= b_{0,1} \otimes (2 b_{1,1} + b_{1,2}) \otimes 1 + (-1-i)\bigl((1+i)b_{0,1} + b_{0,2}\bigr) \otimes b_{1,1} \otimes 1 \\
   &= (2 - 2i)\, E_{1,1,1} + 1\, E_{1,2,1} + (-1 - i)\, E_{2,1,1} + 0\, E_{2,2,1},
   \end{aligned}
   $$
   so Parseval gives
   $$
   C(v, v) = \lvert 2 - 2i \rvert^2 + \lvert 1 \rvert^2 + \lvert -1 - i \rvert^2 + 0 = 8 + 1 + 2 = 11 \gt 0.
   $$
   Evaluating `exists_piForm` directly on the two summands of $v$ gives the same value,
   $$
   C(v, v) = 1 \cdot 5 \cdot 1 + \overline{(-(1-i))}\, (1-i)(2)(-i) + (-(1-i))(1+i)(2)(i) + \lvert -(1-i) \rvert^2 \cdot 3 \cdot 1 \cdot \lvert i \rvert^2 = 5 + 4i - 4i + 6 = 11,
   $$
   and grouping `Fin 3` as head $0$ plus tail $\{1, 2\}$ via `MultiIrr.tensorCons` ($X_0 \otimes_{\mathbb C} (X_1 \otimes_{\mathbb C} X_2) \xrightarrow{\sim} \bigotimes_{a \in \mathrm{Fin}\,3} X_a$) pairs $\psi_2 \otimes 1$ and $\psi_1 \otimes i$ in $X_1 \otimes_{\mathbb C} X_2$ with Gram matrix $\begin{pmatrix} 5 & -2i \\ 2i & 1 \end{pmatrix}$, yielding $1\cdot 5 + (-1-i)(-2i)(1-i) + (-1+i)(2i)(1+i) + 2\cdot 3\cdot 1 = 5 + 4i - 4i + 6 = 11$.
3. *Why finite-support subspaces are chosen per vector rather than globally.* Act by $g = \bigl(\begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1 \\ 1 & 0 \end{pmatrix}, \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 0 \\ 1 & 1 \end{pmatrix}, 3\bigr) \in M$. Right multiplication on $[c : d] \in \mathbb P^1(\mathbb F_3)$ by $w_0 = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1 \\ 1 & 0 \end{pmatrix}$ swaps $C_\infty \leftrightarrow C_0$, while $k_1 = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 0 \\ 1 & 1 \end{pmatrix}$ sends $[c : d] \mapsto [c+d : d]$, fixing $C_\infty$ and cycling $C_0 \to C_1 \to C_2 \to C_0$. Thus $\pi_1(k_1)\psi_2$ has coset values $(\psi_2(C_\infty), \psi_2(C_1), \psi_2(C_2), \psi_2(C_0)) = (3, 1, 1, 3)$, which **leaves $U_1$** (every vector in $U_1 = \operatorname{span}\{\psi_1, \psi_2\}$ takes equal values on $C_\infty$ and $C_0$; acting instead by $\operatorname{diag}(3, 1) \in \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_3)$ even leaves the level-$1$ space $\mathbb C^4$ for level $K_2(3)$). Nevertheless, factorwise invariance $B_0(\pi_0(w_0)\phi_p, \pi_0(w_0)\phi_q) = B_0(\phi_p, \phi_q)$, $B_1(\pi_1(k_1)\psi_p, \pi_1(k_1)\psi_q) = B_1(\psi_p, \psi_q)$, and $\lvert \chi(3) \rvert^2 = \lvert i \rvert^2 = 1$ preserve every pure-tensor factor product, so $C(\pi_\otimes(g)v, \pi_\otimes(g)v) = 11 = C(v, v)$.

---

**Definition 7.3ah (Algebraic unitarisability, the sesquilinear slot flip, and Levi exterior-tensor products).** Let $G, H$ be groups, let $\alpha$ be a finite index set (`[Fintype α]`), let $(\Gamma_a)_{a \in \alpha}$ be groups, and let $X, V, W, X_a$ ($a \in \alpha$) be arbitrary $\mathbb C$-vector spaces (carrying no topology or metric completeness) [Lean, read]:

1. *(Algebraic unitarisability, `IsUnitarisableOfCharIff.lean:305–310`.)* A $\mathbb C$-linear representation $\rho : G \to \mathrm{GL}_{\mathbb C}(X)$ is **unitarisable** (`GlobalTheory.SmGL.IsUnitarisable ρ`) if there exists a map $B : X \to_{\mathbb C} (X \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C)$—$\mathbb C$-linear in its first argument ($B(c v_1 + v_2, w) = c B(v_1, w) + B(v_2, w)$) and conjugate-linear in its second ($B(v, c w_1 + w_2) = \overline{c}\, B(v, w_1) + B(v, w_2)$)—satisfying:
   - *(Hermitian conjugate symmetry `hs`)* $B(w, v) = \overline{B(v, w)}$ for all $v, w \in X$;
   - *(Strict positive definiteness `hp`)* $\operatorname{Re} B(v, v) \gt 0$ for all $v \in X \setminus \{0\}$ (note that `hs` at $w = v$ already forces $B(v, v) = \overline{B(v, v)} \in \mathbb R$, so `hp` is equivalent to $B(v, v) \in \mathbb R_{\gt 0}$ for all $v \ne 0$, while $B(0, 0) = 0$);
   - *(Group invariance `hi`)* $B(\rho(g)v, \rho(g)w) = B(v, w)$ for all $g \in G$ and $v, w \in X$.
   A multiplicative character $\chi : G \to \mathbb C^\times$ is **unitary** (`IsUnitaryChar χ`) if $\lvert \chi(g) \rvert = 1$ for every $g \in G$; on the one-dimensional representation $\operatorname{ofChar}(\chi)$ ($\rho_\chi(g)z = \chi(g)z$ on $\mathbb C$), $\operatorname{IsUnitarisable}(\operatorname{ofChar}(\chi)) \iff \operatorname{IsUnitaryChar}(\chi)$, witnessed by $B(z, w) = z\overline{w}$ (`isUnitarisable_ofChar_iff`, `:311–332`), and pulling $\rho$ back along any group homomorphism $s : H \to G$ preserves unitarisability with the identical form $B$ (`UnitInd.isUnitarisable_comp`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:33–36`).
2. *(The sesquilinear slot flip `UnitInd.core`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:16–30`.)* Mathlib's `InnerProductSpace.Core ℂ X` (`Mathlib/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/Defs.lean:137–156`) follows the functional-analytic convention: conjugate-linear in the first slot (`inner (r • x) y = star r * inner x y`) and $\mathbb C$-linear in the second (`innerₛₗ ℂ : X →ₗ⋆[ℂ] X →ₗ[ℂ] ℂ`). Given $(B, \mathrm{hs}, \mathrm{hp})$ as in (1), `UnitInd.core B hs hp` defines
   $$
   \langle v, w \rangle_{\mathrm{core}} := B(w, v),
   $$
   endowing $X$ via `Core.toNormedAddCommGroup` and `InnerProductSpace.ofCore` with an algebraic `InnerProductSpace ℂ X` structure and norm $\lVert v \rVert = \sqrt{\operatorname{Re} B(v, v)}$ (requiring no completeness on $X$). Flipping the two arguments of `innerₛₗ ℂ` back via $(\operatorname{innerₛₗ}\,\mathbb C).\mathrm{flip}$ recovers $(v, w) \mapsto \langle w, v \rangle_{\mathrm{core}} = B(v, w)$.
3. *(Exterior tensor representations and Levi parabolic products, `IsUnitarisableOfCharIff.lean:131–150`, `LeviEmbMemNormalizerLower.lean:487–559`, `IsSubquotientDetRepParInd.lean:321–323, 341–342, 367–368`.)*
   - For $\rho$ on $(G, V)$ and $\sigma$ on $(H, W)$, the **binary exterior tensor** $\operatorname{extTensor}(\rho, \sigma) = \rho \boxtimes \sigma$ acts on the algebraic tensor product $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ by $(\rho \boxtimes \sigma)(g, h)(v \otimes w) = \rho(g)v \otimes \sigma(h)w$.
   - For $\rho_a$ on $(\Gamma_a, X_a)$ ($a \in \alpha$), the **finite-family exterior tensor** $\operatorname{piTensor}(\rho) = \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} \rho_a$ acts on $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ (`PiTensorProduct ℂ X`) by $\bigl(\bigotimes_a \rho_a\bigr)((g_a)_a)\bigl(\bigotimes_a x_a\bigr) = \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} \rho_a(g_a)x_a$.
   - Over a nonarchimedean local field $k$, for a finite linearly ordered block set $\alpha$, finite decidable coordinate sets $m_a$, a bijection $e : \bigl(\sum_{a \in \alpha} m_a\bigr) \xrightarrow{\sim} n$, and representations $\pi_a$ of $\mathrm{GL}_{m_a}(k)$ on $X_a$, the **finite-block parabolic product** is $\operatorname{multiProd}(e, \pi) := \operatorname{parInd}_e(\operatorname{piTensor}(\pi))$. For two blocks $(r, t)$, letting $\operatorname{Two}(a, b) : \mathrm{Bool} \to \mathrm{Type}$ send $\mathrm{false} \mapsto a$, $\mathrm{true} \mapsto b$ with topological group isomorphism $\operatorname{leviTwo}_{k,a,b} : \mathrm{GL}_a(k) \times \mathrm{GL}_b(k) \xrightarrow{\sim} \operatorname{Levi}_k(\operatorname{Two}(a, b))$, the **two-block parabolic product** is
     $$
     \operatorname{prod}_2(\pi_1, \pi_2) := \operatorname{parInd}_{\operatorname{two}(\operatorname{stdSum})}\Bigl((\pi_1 \boxtimes \pi_2) \circ \operatorname{leviTwo}_{k,\mathrm{Fin}\,r,\mathrm{Fin}\,t}^{-1}\Bigr).
     $$

---

**Lemma 7.3ai (Well-definedness and uniqueness of binary and finite-family algebraic tensor Hermitian forms).**

1. *(Binary tensor sesquilinear lift, `Mathlib/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/TensorProduct.lean:61–75`.)* Let $V, W$ be $\mathbb C$-vector spaces equipped with sesquilinear forms $B_1 : V \to_{\mathbb C} (V \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C)$ and $B_2 : W \to_{\mathbb C} (W \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C)$. There exists a unique map $B_\otimes : (V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W) \to_{\mathbb C} \bigl((V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W) \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C\bigr)$ satisfying
   $$
   B_\otimes(v_1 \otimes w_1, v_2 \otimes w_2) = B_1(v_1, v_2)\, B_2(w_1, w_2) \qquad \text{for all } v_1, v_2 \in V,\ w_1, w_2 \in W.
   $$
   If $B_1$ and $B_2$ are Hermitian conjugate-symmetric (`hs`), then so is $B_\otimes$.
2. *(Finite-family dual-lift construction `exists_piForm` from Hermitian symmetry alone, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:77–93`.)* Let $\alpha$ be a finite set, let $(X_a)_{a \in \alpha}$ be $\mathbb C$-vector spaces, and let $B_a : X_a \to_{\mathbb C} (X_a \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C)$ be sesquilinear forms satisfying Hermitian conjugate symmetry $B_a(w, v) = \overline{B_a(v, w)}$ for all $a \in \alpha$ and $v, w \in X_a$ (with **no** positivity hypothesis). Then there exists a unique map $C : \bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a\bigr) \to_{\mathbb C} \bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C\bigr)$ satisfying
   $$
   C\Bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} x_a,\ \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} y_a\Bigr) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(x_a, y_a) \qquad \text{for all } x, y \in \prod_{a \in \alpha} X_a,
   $$
   and $C(z', z) = \overline{C(z, z')}$ holds for all $z, z' \in \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$. [Lean, read]

*Proof.* (1) Work in the flipped convention $\langle x, y \rangle_1 := B_1(y, x)$, $\langle w, w' \rangle_2 := B_2(w', w)$, so that $\phi_1 := \langle \cdot, \cdot \rangle_1 : V \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} V^*$ and $\phi_2 := \langle \cdot, \cdot \rangle_2 : W \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} W^*$ are both semilinear for the **same** ring endomorphism $\sigma = \mathrm{conj} : \mathbb C \to \mathbb C$, taking values in the $\mathbb C$-linear duals $V^* = \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb C}(V, \mathbb C)$ and $W^* = \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb C}(W, \mathbb C)$. Because $\phi_1$ and $\phi_2$ share the ring endomorphism $\sigma$, $(v, w) \mapsto \phi_1(v) \otimes \phi_2(w) \in V^* \otimes_{\mathbb C} W^*$ satisfies, for every $\lambda \in \mathbb C$,
$$
\phi_1(\lambda v) \otimes \phi_2(w) = \bigl(\overline{\lambda}\phi_1(v)\bigr) \otimes \phi_2(w) = \overline{\lambda}\bigl(\phi_1(v) \otimes \phi_2(w)\bigr) = \phi_1(v) \otimes \bigl(\overline{\lambda}\phi_2(w)\bigr) = \phi_1(v) \otimes \phi_2(\lambda w),
$$
and therefore descends to a conjugate-linear map $\Phi = \operatorname{TensorProduct.map}(\phi_1, \phi_2) : V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} V^* \otimes_{\mathbb C} W^*$ (`LinearAlgebra/TensorProduct/Map.lean:56–90`). Next, applying `mapBilinear` (`:165–183`) and scalar multiplication $\mu_{\mathbb C} : \mathbb C \otimes_{\mathbb C} \mathbb C \to_{\mathbb C} \mathbb C$ produces the canonical $\mathbb C$-linear pairing $\mathcal E : V^* \otimes_{\mathbb C} W^* \to_{\mathbb C} \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb C}(V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W, \mathbb C)$ characterized on pure tensors by $\mathcal E(\ell_1 \otimes \ell_2)(v_2 \otimes w_2) = \ell_1(v_2)\ell_2(w_2)$ (since $(v_2, w_2) \mapsto \ell_1(v_2)\ell_2(w_2)$ is $\mathbb C$-bilinear in $(v_2, w_2)$ and $(\ell_1, \ell_2) \mapsto \bigl(z_2 \mapsto (\ell_1 \otimes \ell_2)(z_2)\bigr)$ is $\mathbb C$-bilinear in $(\ell_1, \ell_2)$). Composing $\mathcal E \circ \Phi$ defines $\langle z_1, z_2 \rangle_\otimes := \mathcal E(\Phi(z_1))(z_2)$ (`TensorProduct.instInner`, `TensorProduct.lean:61–64`), which is conjugate-linear in $z_1$ and $\mathbb C$-linear in $z_2$, and setting $B_\otimes(z_1, z_2) := \langle z_2, z_1 \rangle_\otimes$ gives $B_\otimes(v_1 \otimes w_1, v_2 \otimes w_2) = \langle v_2 \otimes w_2, v_1 \otimes w_1 \rangle_\otimes = B_1(v_1, v_2) B_2(w_1, w_2)$ (`inner_tmul`, `:74–75`). Uniqueness holds because pure tensors span $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$; if `hs` holds for $B_1$ and $B_2$, then on pure tensors $\overline{B_\otimes(v_1 \otimes w_1, v_2 \otimes w_2)} = \overline{B_1(v_1, v_2)}\,\overline{B_2(w_1, w_2)} = B_1(v_2, v_1) B_2(w_2, w_1) = B_\otimes(v_2 \otimes w_2, v_1 \otimes w_1)$, which extends to all $z_1, z_2 \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ by bi-induction on sums.

(2) Following `UnitInd.exists_piForm` (`IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:77–93`), fix a tuple $x = (x_a)_{a \in \alpha} \in \prod_{a \in \alpha} X_a$. For each $a \in \alpha$, the flipped evaluation $(B_a).\mathrm{flip}(x_a) : y_a \mapsto B_a(y_a, x_a)$ is **$\mathbb C$-linear** in $y_a$, because $x_a$ sits in the second slot and $y_a$ sits in the first ($\mathbb C$-linear) slot of $B_a$. Composing the algebra-multiplication multilinear form $\operatorname{mkPiAlgebra} : (a \mapsto c_a) \mapsto \prod_{a \in \alpha} c_a$ on $\mathbb C^\alpha$ with these $\mathbb C$-linear maps yields a $\mathbb C$-multilinear map $y \mapsto \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(y_a, x_a)$ on $\prod_{a \in \alpha} X_a$, which lifts by the universal property `PiTensorProduct.lift` (`PiTensorProduct/Basic.lean:160–215`) to a $\mathbb C$-linear map $\Lambda_x : \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a \to_{\mathbb C} \mathbb C$. Post-composing $\Lambda_x$ with complex conjugation (`starLinearEquiv ℂ`) produces a conjugate-linear functional
$$
F(x) := \overline{\Lambda_x(\,\cdot\,)} \in \Bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a \longrightarrow_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C\Bigr)
$$
(`:80–82`). Evaluating $F(x)$ on any pure tensor $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} y_a$ and applying Hermitian conjugate symmetry `hs` factor by factor gives the key rewrite (`hF`, `:83–85`):
$$
F(x)\Bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} y_a\Bigr) = \overline{\prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(y_a, x_a)} = \prod_{a \in \alpha} \overline{B_a(y_a, x_a)} = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(x_a, y_a).
$$
We claim that $x \mapsto F(x)$ is $\mathbb C$-multilinear from $\prod_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ into the $\mathbb C$-vector space $(\bigotimes_a X_a) \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C$. Indeed, for any coordinate $i \in \alpha$, $u, v \in X_i$, and $c \in \mathbb C$, two conjugate-linear functionals into $\mathbb C$ agree on all of $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ as soon as they agree on the spanning set of pure tensors (`LinearMap.ext_on_range PiTensorProduct.span_tprod_eq_top`, `:87, 89`). On any pure tensor $\bigotimes_a y_a$, the formula $F(x)(\bigotimes_a y_a) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(x_a, y_a)$ is $\mathbb C$-linear in $x_i$ because $B_i(\,\cdot\,, y_i)$ is $\mathbb C$-linear (`MultilinearMap.map_update_add` and `map_update_smul`, `:88, 90`), so $F(x[i \mapsto u + v]) = F(x[i \mapsto u]) + F(x[i \mapsto v])$ and $F(x[i \mapsto c u]) = c\, F(x[i \mapsto u])$ hold on all of $\bigotimes_a X_a$. Applying `PiTensorProduct.lift` a second time lifts $F$ to a $\mathbb C$-linear map $C : \bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a\bigr) \to_{\mathbb C} \bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C\bigr)$ satisfying $C(\bigotimes_a x_a, \bigotimes_a y_a) = F(x)(\bigotimes_a y_a) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(x_a, y_a)$ (`:91–92`). Uniqueness follows from `span_tprod_eq_top` in both arguments, and Hermitian symmetry $C(z', z) = \overline{C(z, z')}$ holds on pure tensors by `hs` and extends to all $z, z'$ by `PiTensorProduct.induction_on` (`ind`, `:99–117`). $\square$

---

**Lemma 7.3aj (Finite-support orthonormalization in arbitrary-dimensional pre-Hilbert spaces).** Let $V$ be any $\mathbb C$-vector space (finite- or infinite-dimensional, with no completeness assumption) equipped with a sesquilinear form $B : V \to_{\mathbb C} (V \to_{\overline{\mathbb C}} \mathbb C)$ satisfying `hs` and `hp`, and let $S \subseteq V$ be a finite subset. Then $U := \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}(S) \subseteq V$ has finite dimension $d = \dim_{\mathbb C} U \in \mathbb N$, and there exist $b_1, \dots, b_d \in U \subseteq V$ such that $B(b_i, b_j) = \delta_{ij}$ for all $1 \le i, j \le d$ and every $u \in U$ (in particular, every $v \in S$) expands uniquely as
$$
u = \sum_{i=1}^d B(u, b_i)\, b_i, \qquad B(u, u') = \sum_{i=1}^d B(u, b_i)\,\overline{B(u', b_i)} \qquad \text{for all } u, u' \in U
$$
(`UnitInd.exists_orthonormal`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:62–74`; `stdOrthonormalBasis`, `Mathlib/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/PiL2.lean:1034–1080` and `Projection/FiniteDimensional.lean:347–418`). [Lean, read]

*Proof.* Since $S$ is finite, $U = \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}(S)$ is finitely generated over the field $\mathbb C$, hence finite-dimensional of some dimension $d = \dim_{\mathbb C} U \in \mathbb N$ (`FiniteDimensional.span_of_finite`, `:69`). Restricting $B$ to $U \times U$ preserves `hs` and `hp` because $U \subseteq V$ is a subspace. We construct an orthonormal basis $b_1, \dots, b_d$ of $U$ by induction on $d \ge 0$. For $d = 0$, $U = \{0\}$ has the empty basis ($0 = \sum_{\emptyset}$). Suppose $d \ge 1$ and the claim holds in dimension $d - 1$. Pick any nonzero $v_0 \in U$. By `hs` and `hp`, $\beta_0 := B(v_0, v_0)$ is a strictly positive real number, so $b_1 := \beta_0^{-1/2} v_0 \in U$ satisfies $B(b_1, b_1) = (\beta_0^{-1/2})^2 B(v_0, v_0) = 1$. Consider the $\mathbb C$-linear functional $\lambda : U \to_{\mathbb C} \mathbb C$, $\lambda(u) := B(u, b_1)$, and set $U' := \ker \lambda = \{u \in U : B(u, b_1) = 0\}$. Because $\lambda(b_1) = 1 \ne 0$, $\lambda$ is surjective onto $\mathbb C$, so rank–nullity gives $\dim_{\mathbb C} U' = d - 1$; moreover, every $u \in U$ decomposes as $u = B(u, b_1) b_1 + \bigl(u - B(u, b_1) b_1\bigr) \in \mathbb C b_1 \oplus U'$. By the induction hypothesis applied to $(U', B|_{U' \times U'})$, $U'$ admits an orthonormal basis $b_2, \dots, b_d \in U'$. Since $b_j \in U'$ ($j \ge 2$) satisfies $B(b_j, b_1) = 0$ and hence $B(b_1, b_j) = \overline{B(b_j, b_1)} = 0$ by `hs`, the tuple $(b_1, b_2, \dots, b_d)$ spans $U$, has cardinality $d = \dim_{\mathbb C} U$, and satisfies $B(b_i, b_j) = \delta_{ij}$. Pairing $u = \sum_{j=1}^d c_j b_j$ against $b_i$ in the second slot gives $B(u, b_i) = \sum_{j=1}^d c_j \delta_{ji} = c_i$, and sesquilinearity gives $B(u, u') = \sum_{i,j=1}^d B(u, b_i)\overline{B(u', b_j)}\delta_{ij} = \sum_{i=1}^d B(u, b_i)\overline{B(u', b_i)}$. (In Lean, `exists_orthonormal` invokes `stdOrthonormalBasis ℂ (Submodule.span ℂ S)` under `UnitInd.core B hs hp`, where `PiL2.lean:759` uses `FiniteDimensional.complete` on the finite-dimensional subspace `span ℂ S`—never on the ambient space $V$—to upgrade `(span v)ᗮ = ⊥` into `span v = ⊤`, while `Projection/FiniteDimensional.lean:380–418` and `GramSchmidtOrtho.lean:64–205` give the inductive splitting and constructive Gram–Schmidt routes.) $\square$

---

**Theorem 7.3ak (Strict positive definiteness, group invariance, and multi-factor compatibility of algebraic tensor forms).**

1. *(Binary exterior-tensor unitarisability, `UnitInd.isUnitarisable_extTensor`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:38–61`; `TensorProduct.lean:77–130`.)* For any groups $G, H$, arbitrary $\mathbb C$-vector spaces $V, W$, and unitarisable representations $(\rho, V)$ of $G$ (with form $B_1$) and $(\sigma, W)$ of $H$ (with form $B_2$), the form $B_\otimes$ of Lemma 7.3ai(1) is Hermitian symmetric, strictly positive definite on all of $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$, and $(G \times H)$-invariant under $\rho \boxtimes \sigma$. In particular, $\operatorname{IsUnitarisable}(\rho \boxtimes \sigma)$ holds.
2. *(Finite-family exterior-tensor unitarisability, `UnitInd.isUnitarisable_piTensor`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:94–150`.)* For any finite index set $\alpha$, groups $(\Gamma_a)_{a \in \alpha}$, arbitrary $\mathbb C$-vector spaces $(X_a)_{a \in \alpha}$, and unitarisable representations $(\rho_a, X_a)$ of $\Gamma_a$ (with forms $B_a$), the form $C$ of Lemma 7.3ai(2) is Hermitian symmetric, strictly positive definite on all of $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$, and $\bigl(\prod_{a \in \alpha} \Gamma_a\bigr)$-invariant under $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} \rho_a$. In particular, $\operatorname{IsUnitarisable}\bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} \rho_a\bigr)$ holds (including when $\alpha = \emptyset$, where $\bigotimes_{a \in \emptyset} X_a \cong \mathbb C$ carries $C(z, w) = z\overline{w}$).
3. *(Compatibility isometries between binary and finite-family tensor forms, `TensorProduct.lean:327–456`, `PiTensorProduct/Basic.lean:830–900`, `IsIrreducibleIffOfLinearEquiv.lean:326–355`.)*
   - *(Two-factor identification $\alpha = \mathrm{Bool}$.)* For $X_{\mathrm{false}} = V$ and $X_{\mathrm{true}} = W$, the canonical $(G \times H)$-equivariant $\mathbb C$-linear isomorphism $\Phi_2 : V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W \xrightarrow{\sim} \bigotimes_{b \in \mathrm{Bool}} X_b$, $\Phi_2(v \otimes w) = \operatorname{tprod}(b \mapsto \text{if } b \text{ then } w \text{ else } v)$, intertwines $B_\otimes$ and $C_{\mathrm{Bool}}$ identically: $C_{\mathrm{Bool}}(\Phi_2(z_1), \Phi_2(z_2)) = B_\otimes(z_1, z_2)$ for all $z_1, z_2 \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$.
   - *(Associativity, symmetry, unit, and `Fin (n+1)` head–tail `tensorCons`.)* The canonical $\mathbb C$-linear equivalences $(E_1 \otimes_{\mathbb C} E_2) \otimes_{\mathbb C} E_3 \xrightarrow{\sim} E_1 \otimes_{\mathbb C} (E_2 \otimes_{\mathbb C} E_3)$ (`assocIsometry`), $E_1 \otimes_{\mathbb C} E_2 \xrightarrow{\sim} E_2 \otimes_{\mathbb C} E_1$ (`commIsometry`), $\mathbb C \otimes_{\mathbb C} E \xrightarrow{\sim} E$ (`lidIsometry`), and the head–tail splitting
     $$
     \operatorname{tensorCons} : X_0 \otimes_{\mathbb C} \Bigl(\bigotimes_{i \in \mathrm{Fin}\,n} X_{i+1}\Bigr) \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} \bigotimes_{j \in \mathrm{Fin}(n+1)} X_j, \qquad x_0 \otimes \Bigl(\bigotimes_{i=0}^{n-1} x_{i+1}\Bigr) \longmapsto \bigotimes_{j=0}^n x_j,
     $$
     preserve the respective tensor Hermitian forms identically: $C_{n+1}(\operatorname{tensorCons}(z_1), \operatorname{tensorCons}(z_2)) = (B_0 \otimes C_{\mathrm{tail}})(z_1, z_2)$. [Lean, read]

*Proof.* We prove (2) first in full generality, then trace the parallel binary path (1) and the compatibility isometries (3).

*Proof of (2).* By Lemma 7.3ai(2), $C$ exists, satisfies $C(\bigotimes_a x_a, \bigotimes_a y_a) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(x_a, y_a)$, and is Hermitian conjugate-symmetric. For $\bigl(\prod_{a \in \alpha} \Gamma_a\bigr)$-invariance (`:144–149`), fix $g = (g_a)_{a \in \alpha} \in \prod_{a \in \alpha} \Gamma_a$. On any pair of pure tensors $\bigotimes_a x_a$ and $\bigotimes_a y_a$,
$$
C\Bigl(\bigl(\bigotimes_a \rho_a\bigr)(g)\Bigl(\bigotimes_a x_a\Bigr),\ \bigl(\bigotimes_a \rho_a\bigr)(g)\Bigl(\bigotimes_a y_a\Bigr)\Bigr) = C\Bigl(\bigotimes_a \rho_a(g_a)x_a,\ \bigotimes_a \rho_a(g_a)y_a\Bigr) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a\bigl(\rho_a(g_a)x_a, \rho_a(g_a)y_a\bigr) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(x_a, y_a) = C\Bigl(\bigotimes_a x_a, \bigotimes_a y_a\Bigr),
$$
using factorwise invariance `hi` of each $B_a$. Since $\bigl(\bigotimes_a \rho_a\bigr)(g)$ is $\mathbb C$-linear and $C$ is sesquilinear, bi-induction on sums and scalar multiples of pure tensors (`ind`, `:99–110`) extends invariance to all $z, z' \in \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$.

Now let $v \in \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ satisfy $v \ne 0$ (`:118–144`). Because pure tensors span $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ (`PiTensorProduct.span_tprod_eq_top`), there exist $N \in \mathbb N$, scalars $r_1, \dots, r_N \in \mathbb C$, and tuples $y_1, \dots, y_N \in \prod_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ such that
$$
v = \sum_{k=1}^N r_k \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} y_{k,a}
$$
(`:118–121`). For each coordinate $a \in \alpha$, the set $S_a := \{y_{1,a}, \dots, y_{N,a}\} \subseteq X_a$ is finite (`Set.finite_range`). Applying Lemma 7.3aj (`exists_orthonormal`, `:122–124`) to $(X_a, B_a, S_a)$ yields an integer $d_a \in \mathbb N$, vectors $b_{a,1}, \dots, b_{a,d_a} \in X_a$ satisfying $B_a(b_{a,p}, b_{a,q}) = \delta_{pq}$, and coefficients $c_{k,a,p} \in \mathbb C$ such that $y_{k,a} = \sum_{p=1}^{d_a} c_{k,a,p}\, b_{a,p}$ for every $1 \le k \le N$. For each multi-index $I = (I_a)_{a \in \alpha} \in \mathcal I := \prod_{a \in \alpha} \{1, \dots, d_a\}$ (a finite index set since $\alpha$ is finite; when $\alpha = \emptyset$, $\mathcal I$ is a singleton $\{!\}$ and $E_! = \operatorname{tprod}(\emptyset)$ spans $\mathbb C$), define the pure tensor
$$
E_I := \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} b_{a, I_a} \in \bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a
$$
(`:125`). Expanding each pure tensor $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} \bigl(\sum_{p=1}^{d_a} c_{k,a,p}\, b_{a,p}\bigr)$ by multilinearity across the finite set $\alpha$ (`MultilinearMap.map_sum` and `MultilinearMap.map_smul_univ`, `:126–133`) places $v$ inside $\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}\{E_I : I \in \mathcal I\}$:
$$
v = \sum_{I \in \mathcal I} t_I\, E_I, \qquad t_I := \sum_{k=1}^N r_k \prod_{a \in \alpha} c_{k,a,I_a} \in \mathbb C.
$$
For any two multi-indices $I, J \in \mathcal I$, Lemma 7.3ai(2) evaluates (`hE`, `:134–136`)
$$
C(E_I, E_J) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} B_a(b_{a, I_a}, b_{a, J_a}) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} \delta_{I_a, J_a} = \begin{cases} 1, & I = J, \\ 0, & I \ne J, \end{cases}
$$
because a finite product of $\{0, 1\}$-factors equals $1$ if and only if $I_a = J_a$ for every $a \in \alpha$ (`Finset.prod_ite_zero`, `funext_iff`). Sesquilinearity of $C$ therefore collapses the double sum for $C(v, v)$ to its diagonal (`h1`, `:137–139`):
$$
C(v, v) = \sum_{I, J \in \mathcal I} t_I \overline{t_J}\, C(E_I, E_J) = \sum_{I \in \mathcal I} \lvert t_I \rvert^2.
$$
Since $v \ne 0$, not all coefficients $t_I$ vanish—otherwise $v = \sum_{I \in \mathcal I} 0 \cdot E_I = 0$, a contradiction (`:140–142`). Choosing $I_0 \in \mathcal I$ with $t_{I_0} \ne 0$ gives $\lvert t_{I_0} \rvert^2 \gt 0$ while every summand $\lvert t_I \rvert^2 \ge 0$, whence $\operatorname{Re} C(v, v) = \sum_{I \in \mathcal I} \lvert t_I \rvert^2 \ge \lvert t_{I_0} \rvert^2 \gt 0$ (`Finset.sum_pos'`, `:143`).

*Proof of (1).* In `isUnitarisable_extTensor` (`IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:38–61`), `UnitInd.core` equips $V$ and $W$ with Mathlib `InnerProductSpace ℂ` structures, and Mathlib's `TensorProduct.lean` (`:77–130`) proves positivity by the exact binary counterpart of the finite-support argument above: for a single $z \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$, `exists_finite_submodule_of_setFinite {z}` (`LinearAlgebra/TensorProduct/Finiteness.lean:106–127`) writes $z$ as a finite sum $\sum_{k=1}^N v_k \otimes w_k$, sets $E' = \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}\{v_1, \dots, v_N\} \subseteq V$ and $F' = \operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}\{w_1, \dots, w_N\} \subseteq W$ (both finite-dimensional), and lifts $z = \iota_{E', F'}(z_0)$ for $z_0 \in E' \otimes_{\mathbb C} F'$, where $\iota_{E', F'} = \operatorname{mapIncl}(E', F') : E' \otimes_{\mathbb C} F' \to V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$. Over the field $\mathbb C$, every subspace admits a $\mathbb C$-linear retraction ($p_E \circ \iota_{E'} = \mathrm{id}_{E'}$, $p_F \circ \iota_{F'} = \mathrm{id}_{F'}$), so $(p_E \otimes p_F) \circ \iota_{E', F'} = \mathrm{id}_{E' \otimes F'}$ and $\iota_{E', F'}$ is injective; thus $z \ne 0 \implies z_0 \ne 0$. Checking pure tensors shows $B_\otimes(\iota_{E', F'}(x), \iota_{E', F'}(y)) = B_{\otimes, E', F'}(x, y)$ (`inner_mapIncl_mapIncl`, `:77–79`). Choosing orthonormal bases $(e_i)$ of $E'$ and $(f_j)$ of $F'$ by Lemma 7.3aj forms the basis $(e_i \otimes f_j)$ of $E' \otimes_{\mathbb C} F'$ (`Basis.tensorProduct`), in which $z_0 = \sum_{i,j} c_{ij}\, e_i \otimes f_j$ has $B_\otimes(z, z) = \sum_{i,j} \lvert c_{ij} \rvert^2 \gt 0$ (`re_inner_self_nonneg` and `inner_definite`, `:98–130`). Double `TensorProduct.induction_on` on $v$ and $w$ (`:50–60`) proves $B_\otimes((\rho \boxtimes \sigma)(g, h)v, (\rho \boxtimes \sigma)(g, h)w) = B_\otimes(v, w)$.

*Proof of (3).* Each listed map is a $\mathbb C$-linear isomorphism built from universal properties (`PiTensorProduct.lift` / `tmulEquivDep` / `Fin.consEquiv` in `IsIrreducibleIffOfLinearEquiv.lean:326–355`, and `TensorProduct.assoc` / `comm` / `lid` in `TensorProduct.lean:327–456`). Because pure tensors span the domain and sesquilinearity extends identities from pure tensors to arbitrary finite sums, it suffices to verify equality on pure tensors. For $\Phi_2$, $\prod_{b \in \mathrm{Bool}} B_b(x_b, y_b) = B_{\mathrm{false}}(v_1, v_2) B_{\mathrm{true}}(w_1, w_2) = B_1(v_1, v_2) B_2(w_1, w_2)$ (`Fintype.prod_bool`). For `assocIsometry` and `commIsometry`, $(b_1 b_2) b_3 = b_1 (b_2 b_3)$ and $b_1 b_2 = b_2 b_1$ in $\mathbb C$ (in Mathlib, verified on norms via `norm_tmul` and upgraded to inner products by polarization `isometry_of_inner`, `Defs.lean:505`). For `tensorCons`, on $z_1 = x_0 \otimes \bigotimes_{i=0}^{n-1} x_{i+1}$ and $z_2 = y_0 \otimes \bigotimes_{i=0}^{n-1} y_{i+1}$,
$$
C_{n+1}\bigl(\operatorname{tensorCons}(z_1), \operatorname{tensorCons}(z_2)\bigr) = \prod_{j=0}^n B_j(x_j, y_j) = B_0(x_0, y_0) \prod_{i=0}^{n-1} B_{i+1}(x_{i+1}, y_{i+1}) = (B_0 \otimes C_{\mathrm{tail}})(z_1, z_2)
$$
by `Fin.prod_univ_succ`. $\square$

---

**Corollary 7.3al (Unitarisability of two-block `prod₂`, finite-block `multiProd`, and equivariant transport).** Let $k$ be a nonarchimedean local field (`IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:482–490`, `NormEqOneOfUnitarisable.lean:31–44`) [Lean, read]:

1. *(Two-block normalized induction `isUnitarisable_prod₂`, `:482–485`.)* For any $r, t \in \mathbb N$ and unitarisable representations $(\pi_1, V)$ of $\mathrm{GL}_r(k)$ and $(\pi_2, W)$ of $\mathrm{GL}_t(k)$, the normalized parabolic product $\operatorname{prod}_2(\pi_1, \pi_2)$ on $\mathrm{GL}_{r+t}(k)$ is smooth and unitarisable.
2. *(Finite-block normalized induction `isUnitarisable_multiProd`, `:487–490`.)* For any finite linearly ordered index set $\alpha$, finite decidable coordinate sets $(m_a)_{a \in \alpha}$, bijection $e : \bigl(\sum_{a \in \alpha} m_a\bigr) \xrightarrow{\sim} n$, and unitarisable representations $(\pi_a, X_a)$ of $\mathrm{GL}_{m_a}(k)$, the normalized parabolic product $\operatorname{multiProd}(e, \pi)$ on $\mathrm{GL}_n(k)$ is smooth and unitarisable.
3. *(Transport across equivariant isomorphisms `isUnitarisable_of_equiv`, `NormEqOneOfUnitarisable.lean:31–44`.)* If $\rho$ and $\sigma$ are $\mathbb C$-representations of a group $G$ connected by a $G$-equivariant $\mathbb C$-linear isomorphism $e : \rho \xrightarrow{\sim} \sigma$, then $\operatorname{IsUnitarisable}(\rho) \implies \operatorname{IsUnitarisable}(\sigma)$ via $B_\sigma(v, w) := B_\rho(e^{-1}v, e^{-1}w)$.

*Proof.* (1) Theorem 7.3ak(1) produces `IsUnitarisable (extTensor π₁ π₂)` on $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ for $\mathrm{GL}_r(k) \times \mathrm{GL}_t(k)$. Pulling back along the group homomorphism $\operatorname{leviTwo}_{k,\mathrm{Fin}\,r,\mathrm{Fin}\,t}^{-1} : \operatorname{Levi}_k(\operatorname{Two}(\mathrm{Fin}\,r, \mathrm{Fin}\,t)) \to \mathrm{GL}_r(k) \times \mathrm{GL}_t(k)$ via `isUnitarisable_comp` (`:33–36`) yields an invariant positive-definite Hermitian form on the two-block Levi representation, and passing that form into the single-Levi compact-integral theorem `isUnitarisable_parInd` of §7.4.3.11 (`:428–480`) proves `IsUnitarisable (prod₂ π₁ π₂)`; smoothness is `isSmooth_prod₂` (`IsSubquotientDetRepParInd.lean:370–371`).

(2) Theorem 7.3ak(2) produces `IsUnitarisable (piTensor π)` directly on $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$ for $\operatorname{Levi}_k(m) = \prod_{a \in \alpha} \mathrm{GL}_{m_a}(k)$, and `isUnitarisable_parInd e` (§7.4.3.11) integrates that tensor form over $K = \mathrm{GL}_n(\mathcal O_k)$ to yield `IsUnitarisable (multiProd e π)`.

(3) Since $e^{-1} : W_\sigma \to V_\rho$ is $\mathbb C$-linear and injective ($v \ne 0 \implies e^{-1}v \ne 0$) and intertwines $\sigma(g)$ with $\rho(g)$ ($e^{-1}(\sigma(g)v) = \rho(g)(e^{-1}v)$), pulling $B_\rho$ back along $e^{-1}$ in both slots preserves sesquilinearity, `hs`, `hp`, and `hi`. $\square$

---

**Remarks, failure boundaries, and formalization notes.**

1. *Strict failure counterexamples when positivity or conjugation hypotheses are dropped* [Expository]. Every hypothesis in Lemma 7.3ai and Theorem 7.3ak is load-bearing:
   - *(Merely positive semidefinite factor $\implies$ nonzero radical tensor.)* On $V = W = \mathbb C^2$, replace $G_1$ in Worked Calculation 1 by the singular Hermitian positive-semidefinite matrix
     $$
     G_1^{\mathrm{psd}} = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 1+i \\ 1-i & 2 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad B_1^{\mathrm{psd}}(x, x) = \bigl\lvert x_1 + (1-i)x_2 \bigr\rvert^2 \ge 0 \quad (\det G_1^{\mathrm{psd}} = 2 - 2 = 0),
     $$
     while keeping $(W, B_2)$ strictly positive definite ($B_2(w_1, w_1) = 4 \gt 0$). For $v_{\mathrm{rad}} = (-1+i, 1)^\top \ne 0$ in $V$, the pure tensor $z_{\mathrm{rad}} = v_{\mathrm{rad}} \otimes w_1$ is **nonzero** in $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ (over the field $\mathbb C$, $v \otimes w = 0 \iff v = 0 \text{ or } w = 0$), yet
     $$
     B_\otimes^{\mathrm{psd}}(z_{\mathrm{rad}}, z_{\mathrm{rad}}) = B_1^{\mathrm{psd}}(v_{\mathrm{rad}}, v_{\mathrm{rad}})\, B_2(w_1, w_1) = \lvert (-1+i) + (1-i) \rvert^2 \cdot 4 = 0 \cdot 4 = 0,
     $$
     so $B_\otimes^{\mathrm{psd}}$ fails strict positive definiteness. (Compare Bernstein 1984, §7.1 Remark, p. 81, separating *semiunitarizable* from *unitarizable* modules.)
   - *(Dropping Hermitian conjugate symmetry `hs` $\implies$ both `exists_piForm` and real diagonals fail.)* On $X_0 = X_1 = \mathbb C^2$, keep $H_1(w, w') = w_1\overline{w'_1} + w_2\overline{w'_2}$ Hermitian on $X_1$, but equip $X_0$ with the sesquilinear form $H_0(x, y) = x^\top \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 2 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix} \overline{y} = x_1\overline{y_1} + 2 x_1\overline{y_2} + x_2\overline{y_2}$, which satisfies $H_0(u, u) = \lvert u_1 \rvert^2 + \lvert u_2 \rvert^2 \gt 0$ on standard basis vectors $u_1, u_2$ yet violates `hs` ($H_0(u_2, u_1) = 0 \ne 2 = \overline{H_0(u_1, u_2)}$). First, at $v = (1, i)^\top \otimes (1, 0)^\top \ne 0$, the diagonal value is non-real: $(H_0 \otimes H_1)(v, v) = (1 + 2(-i) + 1)\cdot 1 = 2 - 2i \notin \mathbb R$. Second, tracing the double-lift formula $F(x)(\bigotimes_a y_a) = \overline{\prod_a H_a(y_a, x_a)}$ inside `exists_piForm` (`:80–86`) at $x = (u_1, w_1)$ and $y = (u_2, w_1)$ produces
     $$
     C_{\mathrm{lift}}(u_1 \otimes w_1, u_2 \otimes w_1) = \overline{H_0(u_2, u_1) H_1(w_1, w_1)} = \overline{0 \cdot 1} = 0 \;\ne\; 2 = H_0(u_1, u_2) H_1(w_1, w_1),
     $$
     because without `hs` the step $\overline{H_0(y_0, x_0)} = H_0(x_0, y_0)$ (`:85`) fails and `exists_piForm` constructs the conjugated transpose $\prod_a \overline{H_a(y_a, x_a)}$ rather than $\prod_a H_a(x_a, y_a)$.
   - *(Dropping conjugate-linearity on one or both factors $\implies$ non-descent or nonzero isotropic tensors.)* If $B_1(z, z') = z\overline{z'}$ is conjugate-linear in slot $2$ on $\mathbb C$ while $B_2(w, w') = w w'$ is $\mathbb C$-bilinear on $\mathbb C$, then the two equal elements $(i \cdot 1) \otimes 1 = 1 \otimes (i \cdot 1)$ of $\mathbb C \otimes_{\mathbb C} \mathbb C$ would pair in the second slot against $1 \otimes 1$ to $B_1(1, i) B_2(1, 1) = -i$ versus $B_1(1, 1) B_2(1, i) = +i \ne -i$; thus $B_1 \cdot B_2$ does not even descend to $\mathbb C \otimes_{\mathbb C} \mathbb C$ (reflecting why `TensorProduct.map` requires the *same* ring endomorphism $\sigma$ on both factors). Even if *both* factors are $\mathbb C$-bilinear symmetric ($\sigma = \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb C}$, e.g. $Q_a(x, y) = x_1 y_1 + x_2 y_2$ on $\mathbb C^2$, which *does* descend to $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$), the nonzero isotropic vector $v_{\mathrm{iso}} = (1, i)^\top \in \mathbb C^2$ satisfies $Q_1(v_{\mathrm{iso}}, v_{\mathrm{iso}}) = 1^2 + i^2 = 0$, giving $(Q_1 \otimes Q_2)(v_{\mathrm{iso}} \otimes w_1, v_{\mathrm{iso}} \otimes w_1) = 0$ on $v_{\mathrm{iso}} \otimes w_1 \ne 0$.

2. *Algebraic tensor product $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ versus Hilbert completed tensor product $V \widehat{\otimes}_2 W$* [Expository]. What is the exact metric boundary of Theorem 7.3ak when $\dim_{\mathbb C} V = \dim_{\mathbb C} W = \infty$?
   - **Proposition 7.3am (Completeness dichotomy for algebraic tensor inner-product spaces).** *Let $(V, B_1)$ and $(W, B_2)$ be nonzero complex pre-Hilbert spaces equipped with the induced norm $\lVert z \rVert_\otimes = \sqrt{B_\otimes(z, z)}$ on the algebraic tensor product $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$.*
     1. *If $\dim_{\mathbb C} V = d \lt \infty$ and $(W, B_2)$ is complete (a Hilbert space), then $(V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W, B_\otimes)$ is already complete, so $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W = V \widehat{\otimes}_2 W$.*
     2. *If $\dim_{\mathbb C} V = \infty$ and $\dim_{\mathbb C} W = \infty$—**even when both $(V, B_1)$ and $(W, B_2)$ are complete Hilbert spaces**—the algebraic tensor product $(V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W, B_\otimes)$ is **incomplete**, and is a proper dense subspace of its metric completion $V \widehat{\otimes}_2 W$.*
   - *Proof.* (1) Choose an orthonormal basis $e_1, \dots, e_d$ of $V$ by Lemma 7.3aj. Every $z \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$ writes uniquely as $z = \sum_{i=1}^d e_i \otimes w_i$ ($w_i \in W$) with $\lVert z \rVert_\otimes^2 = \sum_{i=1}^d B_2(w_i, w_i) = \sum_{i=1}^d \lVert w_i \rVert_W^2$, giving an isometric isomorphism $V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W \cong W^{\oplus d}$, which is complete because $W$ is complete.
     (2) Assume $\dim_{\mathbb C} V = \dim_{\mathbb C} W = \infty$. Choosing linearly independent sequences in $V$ and $W$ and orthonormalizing finite prefixes by Lemma 7.3aj produces countable orthonormal sequences $(e_n)_{n \ge 1}$ in $V$ and $(f_n)_{n \ge 1}$ in $W$. Define the sequence of algebraic tensors
     $$
     z_N := \sum_{n=1}^N \frac{1}{n}\, e_n \otimes f_n \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W \qquad (N \ge 1).
     $$
     For $M \gt N$, orthonormality of $\{e_n \otimes f_m\}$ gives $\lVert z_M - z_N \rVert_\otimes^2 = \sum_{n=N+1}^M \frac{1}{n^2} \le \sum_{n=N+1}^\infty \frac{1}{n(n-1)} = \frac{1}{N} \to 0$ as $N \to \infty$, so $(z_N)_{N \ge 1}$ is Cauchy in $(V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W, \lVert \cdot \rVert_\otimes)$. Suppose for contradiction that $\lVert z_N - z \rVert_\otimes \to 0$ for some $z \in V \otimes_{\mathbb C} W$. By definition of the algebraic tensor product, $z = \sum_{k=1}^r u_k \otimes w_k$ for some finite integer $r \ge 0$ and vectors $u_k \in V$, $w_k \in W$. By Cauchy–Schwarz (`Core.norm_inner_le_norm`, `Defs.lean:254`), for every $n, m \ge 1$, $\lvert B_\otimes(z - z_N, e_n \otimes f_m) \rvert \le \lVert z - z_N \rVert_\otimes \cdot 1 \to 0$, so
     $$
     B_\otimes(z, e_n \otimes f_m) = \lim_{N \to \infty} B_\otimes(z_N, e_n \otimes f_m) = \frac{1}{n}\,\delta_{nm} \qquad \text{for all } n, m \ge 1.
     $$
     Consider the $(r+1) \times (r+1)$ matrix $A = (A_{n,m})_{1 \le n, m \le r+1}$ with entries $A_{n,m} := B_\otimes(z, e_n \otimes f_m)$. Substituting $z = \sum_{k=1}^r u_k \otimes w_k$ factors $A = P Q$, where $P \in \mathbb C^{(r+1) \times r}$ has entries $P_{n,k} = B_1(u_k, e_n)$ and $Q \in \mathbb C^{r \times (r+1)}$ has entries $Q_{k,m} = B_2(w_k, f_m)$. Hence $\operatorname{rank}(A) \le r \lt r + 1$, forcing $\det(A) = 0$. Yet $A = \operatorname{diag}\bigl(1, \tfrac{1}{2}, \dots, \tfrac{1}{r+1}\bigr)$ has $\det(A) = \frac{1}{(r+1)!} \ne 0$ (for example $\frac{1}{24} \ne 0$ when $r = 3$), a contradiction! $\square$
   - *Why `isUnitarisable_parInd` needs only the algebraic tensor product.* In Corollary 7.3al, every smooth section $f \in I_P^G\bigl(\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} \pi_a\bigr)$ has an open right stabilizer in $G$, so its restriction to the compact subgroup $K = \mathrm{GL}_n(\mathcal O_k)$ is locally constant (`isLocallyConstant_indSp`, `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:424–426`) and therefore takes **only finitely many values** in $\bigotimes_{a \in \alpha} X_a$. Thus $x \mapsto C(f(x), h(x))$ is a finite step function on $K$, and no Hilbert-valued Bochner integration or metric completion $V \widehat{\otimes}_2 W$ ever enters the archive's unitarisability proofs. Conversely, completing $(I_P^G(\bigotimes_a \pi_a), C_{\mathrm{ind}})$ in norm recovers the genuine unitary Hilbert-space induction $L^2\text{-}\operatorname{Ind}_P^G\bigl(\widehat{\bigotimes}_a \widehat{X}_a\bigr)$, within which $I_P^G(\bigotimes_a \pi_a)$ is the dense subspace of $G$-smooth ($K$-finite under admissibility) vectors (Bernstein 1984, §5.2 and §5.4, pp. 74–75).

3. *Downstream consumer contract and attribution.* Together with §7.4.3.11, the declarations of `IsLocallyConstantModulus.lean:16–150, 482–490` discharge four families of consumers across the archive (tabulated with exact line ranges and file hashes in the [source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-74312--positive-forms-on-algebraic-exterior-tensors)) [Lean, read]:

   In every one of these callers, Bernstein–Zelevinsky finiteness, Bernstein's irreducibility of unitary parabolic products, the hypotheses of the admissible complement theorem, smooth-dual bijectivity, and complementary-series continuation remain separate hypotheses at their existing grades (`[Lean]`). Classically, smooth pre-unitarizability on algebraic $G$-modules and Levi parabolic products on $\mathrm{GL}_n(F)$ are formulated in Bernstein (1984, §0.2 Corollary p. 51, §§5.2–5.4 pp. 74–75, §§7.1–7.4 pp. 81–83, §8.2 p. 88; [author PDF](https://www.math.tau.ac.il/~bernstei/Publication_list/publication_texts/Bernstein-P-invar-SLN.pdf)) and Casselman (1995 draft, §3.1, Propositions 3.1.1–3.1.4, pp. 32–33; [UBC PDF](https://personal.math.ubc.ca/~cass/research/pdf/p-adic-book.pdf)).
