For arbitrary smooth finite-length $\pi$, its composition factors are smooth and applying $r_b$ gives a filtration with successive quotients their Jacquet modules, some possibly zero. Pull back a composition series from each of those quotients to obtain a finite composition series for $r_b\pi$. This is the induction over the composition series used by the GroundTheory production theorem. [Zelevinsky II, Proposition 1.4](https://www.numdam.org/item/ASENS_1980_4_13_2_165_0.pdf)

*Proof of transport to the companion model.* Choose $\psi:\operatorname{Fin}(|I|)\simeq I$ and $e_0=\psi^{-1}e$. Matrix reindexing sends $g$ to the matrix with entries $g_{\psi^{-1}(i),\psi^{-1}(j)}$. Pullback by this continuous isomorphism preserves smoothness by preimages of stabilizers and preserves the invariant lattice by surjectivity. Take the natural rank in $\alpha$ of the block label of each coordinate. This preserves strict comparisons and, by trichotomy, equality. The lower zero equations and the diagonal identity equations therefore identify the two parabolics and the two radicals. Each Levi tuple embeds in the labelled Levi. Conversely, take the diagonal blocks of a labelled Levi matrix. They are invertible and their embedding reconstructs the matrix. This is the Levi surjection used by the bridge.

Apply the GroundTheory theorem to the pulled-back representation. Undo its inverse square-root twist. Multiplication of each group operator by a nonzero scalar preserves its invariant subspaces in both directions. Pull the resulting action back along the Levi surjection, which also preserves the invariant lattice. On quotient classes send $[v]\mapsto[v]$ from the GroundTheory coinvariants to the companion quotient by the span of the character-$1$ relations. Every source relation belongs to the target span, and the map is onto by representatives. It intertwines the two Levi actions on each $[v]$. Length passes to the quotient. Entrywise reindexing then transports the radical relations and Levi actions from $e_0$ to $e$, with the identity on the Levi tuple. Apply the companion inverse square-root twist. [Pinned declaration and consumer table](#normalized-jacquet-finite-length-supply)

**Remarks.** To test the finite-length hypothesis, take $\bigoplus_{j\ge1}\mathbf 1_G$ with its algebraic direct-sum action. It is smooth, and for the upper Borel of $\mathrm{GL}_2$ its normalized Jacquet module is $\bigoplus_{j\ge1}(\nu^{-1/2}\boxtimes\nu^{1/2})$. The partial sums give a strict infinite chain. For a control on the smooth-category conclusion, take an abstract field embedding $\iota:\mathbb Q_p\hookrightarrow\mathbb C$, obtained by extending an embedding of a transcendence basis to an algebraic extension, and use it on the natural two-dimensional representation of the abstract group. A line invariant under each of the upper and lower elementary unipotents would have to be both of their distinct fixed lines, and the upper radical relation span is exactly the first coordinate line. Before normalization the quotient has character $(a,d)\mapsto\iota(d)$. On $\operatorname{diag}(1,d)$ with $d\in\mathbb Z_p^\times$ the normalized character is still $\iota(d)$, whose kernel there is trivial and fails to be open. This tests smoothness of the result while retaining algebraic length one.

Length measures the number of simple factors. The Steinberg constituent in Example 2 is infinite-dimensional, as disjoint compact open balls give arbitrarily large subspaces of locally constant functions on $\mathbb P^1(F)$ modulo constants. For a nonsplit finite-length example, let $g$ act by the matrix with diagonal entries $1$ and upper entry $v_F(\det g)$. It is smooth. Every unipotent radical acts trivially and the same Jordan action survives with the common normalizing twist. A matrix with determinant $\varpi$ has a nontrivial Jordan block, so the invariant line has no invariant complement. The theorem uses the radical quotient with trivial radical character. The highest mirabolic derivative uses its own nonvanishing and irreducibility arguments.

**Scope of the source reading.** The normalized geometric lemma supplies the cell filtration for smooth complex inducing data. The inner modular comparison in `GeometricLemmaNormal.lean:203–218` is a named input. The projective and injective steps use `CuspidalSplittingProjective.lean:39–43,195–199` for a smooth irreducible with vanishing proper Jacquet modules and a prescribed scalar central action. The deeper matrix-coefficient step is named. The separate cuspidal admissibility result in `CuspidalAdmissible.lean:271–481` uses Cartan decomposition and contraction at a principal congruence level. In the large-gap case the diagonal conjugation sends the finite family of radical witnesses into the averaging subgroup. In the bounded-gap case removing one scalar per block leaves finitely many exponent lists. The length caller uses the embedding and the cuspidal vanishing and drops the admissibility field. The distinct fixed-vector lift for an admissible input and a compact open subgroup with Iwahori factorization is recorded in the declaration table. The proof-body reading is author-level local R3. Lean kernel replay, Main proof-term reachability and independent review are pending. [Declaration and consumer table](#normalized-jacquet-finite-length-supply)

**Consumers and attribution.** The Bernstein–Zelevinsky finiteness bundle puts this result in the second of four fields. The cell multiplicity theorem projects that field for each row with the smoothness and finite-length hypotheses of that row. Its finite product $T'$ contains the tuples of row constituent classes. Outside $T'$ some row multiplicity is zero. The inherited exterior-tensor classification reindexes $T'$ by block factors. To obtain finite support of the *output* irreducible classes and natural-number column multiplicities in general, the argument also uses the column finite-length supply of §7.4.3.6.

The classical result with proof is already Zelevinsky II, Proposition 1.4, in the normalization of BZ I. BZ I, §§2.8 and 2.11–2.14, supply the cuspidal induction and detection argument that Zelevinsky combines with the geometric lemma and exactness. This section explains the Lean production by Alpöge and Claude, including its finite labelling family and explicit quotient transport. [Source and attribution record](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-normalized-jacquet-finite-length)

#### 7.4.3.20 From positive semidefinite forms to the strict lower-derivative bound

A unitary representation has scalar eigenvalues of modulus one at its centre. Its lower derivatives can have quite different scalar moduli, because the derivative is a quotient by unipotent relations and its natural action carries a determinant shift. The strict bound comes from two facts. A higher nonzero derivative forces the ordinary averaging limit at the current level to vanish. A scalar eigenvector at that level has an averaged norm which could fail to decay only if the scalar reached or exceeded the proposed bound.

For a first look, take the normalized principal series $1\times\eta$ of $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Q_2)$, where $\eta(x)=\mathrm i^{v_2(x)}$. Its first derivative has two one-dimensional graded pieces with scalar values $2^{-1/2}$ and $\mathrm i2^{-1/2}$ at $2I_1$. Its second derivative is a line on $\mathrm{GL}_0$, on which the scalar is $1$. The strict inequality belongs to the first derivative because the second derivative survives. In rank three an order-two lower derivative already involves the nonzero shift $-1/2$. We compute both examples below.

**Derivatives, levels and averages.** Let $F$ be a nonarchimedean local field, with normalized absolute value $a=|z|_F$ on $F^\times$, and let $\psi:F\to S^1$ be any continuous nontrivial additive character. Write $G_r=\mathrm{GL}_r(F)$, $G_0=\{1\}$ and $\nu(g)=|\det g|_F$. Use upper-left embeddings $g\mapsto\operatorname{diag}(g,I)$ and the mirabolic $P_r$ fixing the last row of the identity. For a smooth complex $G_N$-module $(\pi,V)$, $D_i(\pi)$ is the unnormalized derivative. For $i\ge1$ it restricts to $P_N$, takes $i-1$ successive last-column coinvariants with $\psi$ on the bottom coordinate of the column, and then takes ordinary last-column coinvariants. Put $D_0(\pi)=\pi$. The notation $D_i(\pi)(t)$ multiplies the action by $\nu^t$ on the same carrier.

Use zero-based matrix indices. Let $U_b$ be the upper unitriangular subgroup in $G_N$ whose first $b$ columns are those of the identity. Let $K_b$ be the span of $\pi(u)v-\psi(\sum_{r\ge b}u_{r,r+1})v$ for $u\in U_b$ and $v\in V$, with the sum restricted to entries of the matrix. Let $R_b$ use the sum starting at $\max(b-1,0)$ instead. Thus $R_N=0$. The two characters are `derChar` and `lvlChar`. For $i=j+1$, $N=n+i$, the grouped quotient dictionary gives a surjective linear map
$$
q:V\longrightarrow D_i(\pi),\qquad \ker q=K_n,
\qquad q(\pi(\operatorname{diag}(g,I_i))v)=D_i(\pi)(g)q(v).
$$
Here $D_i(\pi)(g)$ denotes the untwisted action. This uses the quotient comparison of §7.4.3.18 with its Levi action. [Grouped derivatives](#74318-from-successive-mirabolic-quotients-to-one-grouped-derivative) In particular $D_i(\pi)=0$ exactly when $K_n=V$, compatibly with the rank identifications. This follows from surjectivity and the kernel identity. The dictionary's map is the composite of the $j$ twisted quotient maps and the final ordinary quotient map.

For $b\le a_0$, $U_{a_0}$ is a subgroup of $U_b$. On $U_{a_0}$ a superdiagonal entry with row index $r<a_0-1$ has column index $r+1<a_0$ and is zero. Any two starting indices less than $a_0$ therefore give the same character on $U_{a_0}$. By inclusion of the spans of the relations, we have
$$
R_{a_0}\subset R_b,\qquad R_{a_0}\subset K_b\quad(b<a_0).
$$
The first inclusion here also allows $b=a_0$. This argument uses the original relation spaces on $V$.

A sesquilinear form is linear in its first variable. Write $\ker B=\{v:B(v,w)=0\text{ for every }w\}$. A positive semidefinite form is Hermitian with real diagonal entries $\ge0$. A level form of weight $s$ at $m$ ($m+2\le N$) is such a form $B$ that kills $R_{m+2}$ in the first variable and satisfies
$$
B(\pi(p)v,\pi(p)w)=|\det p|_F^s B(v,w)\qquad(p\in P_{m+2})
$$
under upper-left embedding. This is `Lvl`. In this mirabolic let $u(x)$, $x\in F^{m+1}$, fill the column of index $m+1$ above its diagonal. For a compact open additive subgroup $A\subset F^{m+1}$, let $P_A$ be the ordinary probability average of $\pi(u(x))$ and $P_{A,\psi}$ the average weighted by $\overline{\psi(x_m)}$. Both are finite sums on each vector. For the twisted average use a common open subgroup of $A$ fixing the vector and in the kernel of the character. The character has open kernel, since a sufficiently small arc about $1$ in $S^1$ contains no nontrivial subgroup and $F$ has a basis of open additive subgroups. Compactness gives finitely many cosets. Refining the subgroup repeats each summand with the same multiplicity, giving the same normalized sum.

The relation `IsAtom` between $B$ and $B_0$ is $B(P_Av,w)\to B_0(v,w)$ for each pair as $A$ increases by inclusion. The relation `IsDens` between $B$ and $B'$ at a fixed compact open additive subgroup $A_0$ is
$$
B'(v,w)=[A:A_0] B(P_{A,\psi}v,w)
$$
for every compact open $A$ containing a sufficiently large $A_1\supset A_0$ depending on $v,w$. The sums of two compact open additive subgroups are compact and open, so one can impose two eventual identities at once.

**The inputs to the assembly.** The following form inputs apply for each such $\psi$, every $m,N$ with $m+2\le N$, every smooth complex $G_N$-module, every real $s$ and every level form $B$ as above. The density, splitting and lifting statements use a compact open additive subgroup $A_0$ of $F^{m+1}$.

| Input | Object or implication |
|---|---|
| `UAtom` | Supplies an atom $B_0$ in the stated limit relation. It is positive semidefinite, is dominated by $B$ on the diagonal, kills $K_{m+1}$ and has determinant covariance of weight $s$ both on the embedded $G_{m+1}$ and on $P_{m+2}$. |
| `UDens` | Supplies a density $B'$ with the stated eventual identities. It is positive semidefinite, kills $R_{m+1}$ and has covariance of weight $s+1$ on $P_{m+1}$. If $B'(v,v)=0$, then $v\in R_{m+1}+\ker B$. |
| `USplit` | For $B_0,B'$ satisfying the two relations with $B$, if the entire diagonal of $B'$ is zero then $B_0=B$. If $B_0=c_0 B$ and some $B'(v,v)\ne0$, then $c_0=0$. |
| `ULift` | Given the density $B'$ and a positive semidefinite $C$ killing $R_{m+1}$, with covariance of weight $s+1$ on $P_{m+1}$ and diagonal dominated by $B'$, supplies a weight-$s$ level form $I$ at $m$, dominated by $B$, with density $C$ at the same $A_0$ and atom zero. |
| `UShell` | For $B$ with atom zero, every $z\in F^\times$ with $a<1$, $c\in\mathbb C$ and $v\in V$ such that $\pi(\operatorname{diag}(zI_{m+1},I))v-cv\in K_{m+1}$ and $v\notin K_{m+1}+\ker B$ satisfy $|c|^2<a^{(m+1)s}$. The proof below gives the local shell estimate. |

The input `ThmB` is the proportionality of mirabolic-invariant forms. For every rank $N\ge1$, a smooth irreducible $\pi$ of $G_N$ with a fixed invariant positive definite Hermitian form $H$ has the following property. Every sesquilinear form invariant under $P_N$ equals $c_0H$ for some $c_0\in\mathbb C$. It is the form interface in the archive.

The input `UEig` applies to every $\psi$ as above, $n,i\ge0$, smooth irreducible $\pi$ of $G_{n+i}$ and real $t$. If a nonzero subquotient $(\sigma,W)$ of $D_i(\pi)(t)$ has scalar character $\omega:F^\times\to\mathbb C^\times$, then for each $z\in F^\times$ the shifted action at $zI_n$ has an eigenvector $x\ne0$ in the whole derivative with eigenvalue $\omega(z)$. A subquotient here comes with an invariant subspace $S$ and a surjective intertwiner $f:S\to W$. The separate interface `UAdm` supplies admissibility of $D_i(\pi)$ for the same smooth irreducible inputs and all $i$. Admissibility consists of smoothness and finite-dimensional fixed spaces for compact open subgroups. The concrete `uEig` uses `uAdm` internally, as follows. Lift $0\ne w\in W$ to $y\in S$. In the untwisted derivative choose a compact open $J$ fixing $y$, and set $E=D_i(\pi)^J$. Centrality makes $E$ stable under $T=D_i(\pi)(t)(zI_n)$, since multiplying an operator by a scalar preserves that stability. On the finite-dimensional space $E\cap S$, $T-\omega(z)$ takes values in $\ker f$. It misses $y$ and so has a nonzero kernel. A vector from this kernel gives the required $x$. The `uAdm` declaration uses ordinary irreducible admissibility at order zero and derivative smoothness and finite length, with irreducible admissibility of the constituents, at positive orders.

**Lemma (The form at each level) [Lean, read].** Start with $C_0=H$ on $V$, where $H$ is invariant positive definite Hermitian. Make $C_{d+1}$ the density of $C_d$ at the standard lattice $\mathcal O_F^{N-1-d}$ whenever $d+2\le N$. For such $d$, $C_d$ is a level form at $m=N-2-d$ with weight $d$. For every $d+1\le N$,
$$
C_d(v,v)=0\ \Longrightarrow\ v\in R_{N-d}.
$$
If some $C_{d+1}(v,v)\ne0$, the atom of $C_d$ vanishes as a form, assuming `UAtom`, `USplit`, `ULift` and `ThmB` for the original smooth irreducible representation.

*Proof.* The standard lattices are finite products of the compact open unit ball. At $d=0$ the form is positive semidefinite, kills $R_N=0$ and has the required weight-zero covariance. At each successor the density supplies semidefiniteness, annihilation of $R_{N-d-1}$ and covariance on $P_{N-d-1}$ with weight $d+1$. These give the next level whenever that level has a following column. They also apply at $d+1=N-1$. For the diagonal implication the base case is positive definiteness. At the successor `UDens` places a diagonal-zero vector in $R_{N-d-1}+\ker C_d$. An element of $\ker C_d$ has zero diagonal, hence belongs to $R_{N-d}$ by induction, which is included in $R_{N-d-1}$. This proves the implication for all indices stated.

For proportionality, at index $\ell$ with $\ell+1\le N$ consider any semidefinite form $C$ that kills $R_{N-\ell}$, has covariance of weight $\ell$ on $P_{N-\ell}$ and is dominated on the diagonal by $C_\ell$. At $\ell=0$, `ThmB` makes it proportional to $H$. At $\ell=d+1$, `ULift` with parent $C_d$ supplies $I$ with density $C$ and the conditions for the same assertion at $d$. Write $I=c_0C_d$ by induction. For each $v,w$, use one common lattice in the eventual equalities for $I$ and $C_d$. The identical index factor and first-variable linearity give $C(v,w)=c_0 C_{d+1}(v,w)$. This proves the proportionality at every level.

The atom of $C_d$ meets these conditions at $\ell=d$: `UAtom` gives covariance on $P_{N-d}$, diagonal domination and annihilation of $K_{N-d-1}\supset R_{N-d}$. It follows that the atom equals $c_0 C_d$. When a diagonal value of $C_{d+1}$ is nonzero, the second clause of `USplit` gives $c_0=0$. The first clause concerns the separate case in which the entire next diagonal vanishes. $\square$

**Lemma (The strict bound from expanding balls) [Lean, read].** The `UShell` estimate holds with the level, atom and vector hypotheses in the table.

*Proof.* Write $n=m+1$, $g=\operatorname{diag}(zI_n,I)$, $\lambda=a^{ns}>0$ and $L=R_{n+1}$. Let $M$ be the span of $\pi(u(x))y-y$ for $x\in F^n$ and $y\in V$. We have $K_n\subset L+M$ and the column group preserves $L$. Indeed, an element $U\in U_n$ factors as $u(x)U'$ by clearing column $n$ above the diagonal, with $U'\in U_{n+1}$. The character defining $K_n$ agrees on this factorization with the character defining $L$ on $U'$, since it starts at row $n$. The corresponding twisted relation decomposes into a column translate of an $L$-relation and a scalar multiple of an ordinary column difference. Conjugation by $u(x)$ preserves $U_{n+1}$ and its character. It preserves the first $n+1$ columns because $u(x)$ and its inverse carry their span into itself. On superdiagonal entries with row at least $n$, left multiplication changes only earlier rows and right multiplication changes only column $n$. Thus it preserves the relation span $L$. Also $M\subset K_n$ because the derivative character is trivial on $u(x)$.

Write $\pi(g)v=cv+w_1+w_2$ with $w_1\in L$ and $w_2\in M$. Express $w_2$ as a finite sum of column differences with coefficients. Take an integer $b\ge0$ so that $A_\ell=z^{-(\ell+b)}\mathcal O_F^n$ contains all translations in those differences for $\ell\ge0$. Each $P_{A_\ell}$ then kills $w_2$ by permutation of cosets under each translation. These finite sums can use a common stabilizer inside $A_\ell$ for all vectors in each calculation. We have $P_{A_\ell}w_1\in L$. The ordinary average is self-adjoint for $B$ because the column subgroup acts isometrically and inversion permutes the cosets. It is also idempotent because its image is fixed by the whole subgroup being averaged. Consequently
$$
 B(P_{A_\ell}\pi(g)v,\pi(g)v)=|c|^2 B(P_{A_\ell}v,v).
$$
In this expansion the averaged error in the first variable has zero pairing, and the error in the second variable has zero pairing by self-adjointness and Hermitian symmetry.

Since $g u(x)g^{-1}=u(zx)$ and $zA_{\ell+1}=A_\ell$, transporting the finite average gives $P_{A_\ell}\pi(g)=\pi(g)P_{A_{\ell+1}}$. The level covariance then gives $\lambda B(P_{A_{\ell+1}}v,v)$ as another expression for the same value. With $b_\ell=B(P_{A_\ell}v,v)$ we get
$$
\lambda b_{\ell+1}=|c|^2 b_\ell,\qquad
b_\ell=B(P_{A_\ell}v,P_{A_\ell}v)\ge0.
$$
The normalized absolute value condition $a<1$ makes these balls increasing and cofinal among compact open additive subgroups. Each coordinate of a compact subgroup is bounded, so the entire subgroup fits in a sufficiently large ball. The atom-zero condition gives $b_\ell\to0$. If $|c|^2\ge\lambda$ then $b_\ell\ge b_0$ for all $\ell$, so $b_0=0$. Equality in the proposed bound is included: it would make the sequence constant.

For a positive semidefinite form, $B(y,y)=0$ implies $y\in\ker B$. To see this, fix $w$, put $b'=B(y,w)$, $r=B(w,w)\ge0$ and $t=1/(1+r)$. The diagonal at $y-tb'w$ is $(t^2r-2t)|b'|^2$. Its coefficient is strictly negative since $tr<1$. Nonnegativity for every $w$ forces $b'=0$. Apply this to $y=P_{A_0}v$, with $A_0$ here the first ball of the sequence. The vector $v-P_{A_0}v$ is a finite average of ordinary column differences and thus is in $K_n$. This puts $v$ in $K_n+\ker B$, contrary to the vector hypothesis. $\square$

**Theorem (Strict modulus for a positive non-highest derivative) [Lean, read].** Assume the seven inputs `UAtom`, `UDens`, `USplit`, `ULift`, `UShell`, `UEig` and `ThmB` just stated. For every such $F,\psi$, all $n\ge0$, $i\ge1$ and every smooth irreducible unitarisable complex $G_{n+i}$-module $\pi$, suppose there are $n',h'\ge0$ and a rank identification $n+i=n'+h'$ with $h'>i$ and $D_{h'}(\pi)\ne0$. For every nonzero complex representation $(\sigma,W)$ that is a subquotient of $D_i(\pi)((1-i)/2)$ and every homomorphism $\omega:F^\times\to\mathbb C^\times$ satisfying $\sigma(zI_n)=\omega(z)\operatorname{id}_W$ for all $z$, we have
$$
|\omega(z)|<1\qquad (z\in F^\times,\ |z|_F<1).
$$

*Proof.* Write $i=j+1$ and $N=n+i$. The higher order forces $n'<n$, in particular $n\ge1$. Fix a positive definite invariant form $H$ on $\pi$. By the grouped quotient and rank transport, $K_{n'}\ne V$. If all diagonal values of $C_{j+1}$ were zero, the level lemma would put all of $V$ in $R_{N-j-1}=R_n$. Since $R_n\subset K_{n'}$, this would give $K_{n'}=V$. Thus the next density has a nonzero diagonal value. The atom of $C_j$ is zero, and $C_j$ has level $m=n-1$ with weight $j$.

Fix $z$ as in the statement. Set $t=(1-i)/2=-j/2$. Apply `UEig` to obtain a nonzero vector in the shifted derivative of eigenvalue $\omega(z)$, and write it as $q(v)$ by surjectivity. The shift multiplies the scalar action by
$$
d_t=|\det(zI_n)|_F^t=a^{nt}>0.
$$
Thus $c=d_t^{-1}\omega(z)$ is the eigenvalue in the untwisted derivative on the same vector. Equivariance of $q$ gives
$$
\pi(\operatorname{diag}(zI_n,I_i))v-cv\in K_n.
$$
Also $\ker C_j\subset R_{N-j}=R_{n+1}\subset K_n$. For the first inclusion, membership in the left kernel gives zero diagonal, and the level lemma applies. Because $q(v)\ne0$, $v$ is outside $K_n+\ker C_j=K_n$. This uses a containment of kernels and leaves open the possibility of nonzero $K_n$ and of a degenerate $C_j$.

Now `UShell` with $m=n-1$ and $s=j$ gives $|c|^2<a^{nj}$. Since the determinant is $z^n$ and $2nt=-nj$, the factor $d_t$ has squared modulus $a^{-nj}$. Multiplying the strict inequality by this positive factor gives
$$
 |\omega(z)|^2=|c|^2 a^{-nj}<a^{nj}a^{-nj}=1.
$$
The nonnegative modulus is therefore strictly less than one. $\square$

**Two calculations on actual representations [Expository].** Use $F=\mathbb Q_2$, $z=2$, $a=1/2$, and $\psi(x)=\exp(2\pi\mathrm i\,\{x\}_2)$ where $\{x\}_2$ is the finite negative-power part of the dyadic expansion modulo integers. Then $\psi(1/2)=-1$. Set $\chi_e(x)=\mathrm i^{e v_2(x)}$. Each $\chi_e$ is a smooth unitary line. For a product of such characters in rank $r$, normalized Borel induction gives a smooth module with covariance $\delta_B^{1/2}\prod_k\chi_{e_k}(a_k)$ on diagonal $(a_1,\ldots,a_r)$. It has a section taking value $1$ at the identity, obtained by extending the covariance on $BK$ with right-$K$ invariance for $K=G_r(\mathbb Z_2)$. This is well-defined because the multiplier is trivial on $B\cap K$ and $BK=G_r$ by Iwasawa decomposition. The normalized unitary induction form is the integral of $f(k)\overline{h(k)}$ over $K$ with total mass one. The product of two section values has covariance $\delta_B$ because the inducing character is unitary. The quotient integration formula makes the integral of such a density over the space of left $B$-cosets invariant under right translation. Positivity is strict for a nonzero smooth section, since its restriction to $K$ is nonzero on an open set. Zelevinsky's singleton-segment criterion gives irreducibility when no ratio of two inducing characters equals $|\cdot|_F$ or $|\cdot|_F^{-1}$, as is the case for the unitary phase characters below. [Zelevinsky II, §4.2](https://www.numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1379/)

Each rank-one factor has $D_0=\chi_e$ and $D_1=\mathbb C$ on the trivial group, since its mirabolic and unipotent radical are trivial. Apply the Leibniz filtration of the product in the unnormalized derivative convention. For an order-$i$ derivative of the $r$ factors the graded pieces correspond to deleting $i$ distinct characters. Each remaining block carries a twist of exponent $i/2$. This follows by induction from the binary cross twists: if the last factor is deleted, every earlier surviving factor picks up an extra $1/2$, and otherwise the last surviving factor picks up $i/2$ while the earlier surviving factors keep their previous $i/2$. In passing from an earlier filtration to a binary layer with the last factor, normalized parabolic induction is exact and induction in stages gives the indicated Borel induction. Each piece is nonzero by the same section construction, including empty induction as the line on $G_0$. At $zI_{r-i}$ the parabolic modulus is one. The scalar of the untwisted piece with remaining index set $J$ is $a^{(r-i)i/2}\prod_{k\in J}\chi_{e_k}(z)$. The shift multiplies it by $a^{(r-i)(1-i)/2}$, leaving $a^{(r-i)/2}\prod_{k\in J}\chi_{e_k}(z)$. [Bernstein–Zelevinsky I, §§1.9, 3.5, 4.5, 4.14](https://www.numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1333/)

- **Rank two, $1\times\chi_1$.** Order zero has scalar $\mathrm i$ at $2I_2$. At order one $n=1$, $j=t=0$. The two pieces on $G_1$ have characters $\nu^{1/2}$ and $\nu^{1/2}\chi_1$. Their scalars at $2$ are $1/\sqrt2$ and $\mathrm i/\sqrt2$. Their squared moduli are $1/2<1=a^{nj}$. At order two there is a single nonzero quotient, the whole line on $G_0$. Its shift is trivial on that group and the scalar is $1$. This proves that order one is non-highest and order two is highest, with the empty determinant equal to one.
- **Rank three, $1\times\chi_1\times\chi_2$.** Order zero has scalar $-\mathrm i$ at $2I_3$. At order one the remaining pairs $(0,1),(0,2),(1,2)$ of character exponents give central values $\mathrm i/2,-1/2,-\mathrm i/2$ at $2I_2$. Their shift is zero and their squared moduli are $1/4<1$. At order two there are three lines on $G_1$. For the remaining exponents $0,1,2$, the unshifted values $c$ at $2$ are $1/2,\mathrm i/2,-1/2$. Thus $|c|^2=1/4<1/2=a^{nj}$ with $n=j=1$. Here $t=-1/2$, $d_t=\sqrt2$, so the shifted values are $1/\sqrt2,\mathrm i/\sqrt2,-1/\sqrt2$, and their squared moduli are $1/2<1$. Reversing the shift would give squared moduli $1/8$ and would fail to give the normalized values. At order three there is exactly one nonzero piece, the line on $G_0$ with scalar $1$. Both lower orders have a higher nonzero derivative.

Each piece in these calculations is a genuine nonzero subquotient with a central character on all $F^\times$. At the specified $z$, the scalars in a given positive order are distinct whenever there is more than one piece. If $T$ is the scalar-matrix action and the graded scalars are $c_1,\ldots,c_d$, the filtration gives $\prod_k(T-c_k)=0$. For a lift of a nonzero class in the $c_k$ piece, applying $\prod_{\ell\ne k}(T-c_\ell)/(c_k-c_\ell)$ fixes that class and gives a nonzero $c_k$-eigenvector in the whole derivative. Thus these values also occur on actual eigenvectors before passage to a subquotient.

**What the hypotheses exclude.** For a counterexample to removing positive definiteness, take $\nu^{-1}\times\nu$ on $G_2$ over the same field, with $\nu$ on each rank-one block equal to the absolute value. Its singleton segments are unlinked, since the ratio is $\nu^{-2}$, so the principal series is smooth irreducible. It has a nondegenerate invariant Hermitian form. Indeed, its smooth Hermitian dual is $\nu\times\nu^{-1}$ by the compact-picture pairing of normalized induction, and the two irreducible products are isomorphic by equality of composition factors under exchange. To see the pairing, integrate the product of sections on $K$ with the dual inducing characters. The product transforms by $\delta_B$, and the integral is invariant. Sections in the dual compact picture separate each nonzero section by a compact open patch, so the induced isomorphism with the Hermitian dual yields a nonzero invariant sesquilinear form. At least one of its Hermitian and skew-Hermitian parts (dividing the latter by $\mathrm i$) is nonzero. The radical of that Hermitian form is invariant and is zero by irreducibility. The second derivative is again a nonzero line and the first has a piece $\nu^{-1/2}$, with $|\omega(2)|^2=2$. This also shows that the invariant nondegenerate Hermitian form must be indefinite: on an irreducible representation, any nonzero invariant semidefinite form, after changing sign if necessary, is definite by the diagonal-zero lemma and invariance of the radical. A positive definite form would give the theorem's bound. If the weakening permits a zero positive semidefinite form, this example has that form as well. If one still requires a **nonzero** invariant positive semidefinite form on the irreducible original representation, the radical argument recovers positive definiteness and the conclusion. [Zelevinsky II, §1.9 and Theorem 4.2](https://www.numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1379/)

For deletion of original irreducibility take $(1\times\chi_1)\oplus\mathbf 1_{G_2}$ with the direct sum positive definite form. Derivatives preserve this finite direct sum because each successive relation span is the sum of the corresponding spans in the two summands. On the determinant line $\mathbf 1_{G_2}$ the ordinary first quotient retains the line on $G_1$, with modulus $1$ at $2$, while the first twisted step of $D_2$ imposes $v-\psi(1/2)v=2v$ and kills the line. The principal-series summand supplies $D_2\ne0$ for the whole module. Thus order one is below the highest for the whole module and still has the modulus-one subquotient. The projection onto the trivial summand gives an invariant form on the direct sum supported on just that summand, which also shows precisely how mirabolic proportionality fails when its original irreducibility premise is removed.

At order zero the theorem's premise $i\ge1$ fails. The unshifted $D_0$ in the unitary examples has unit central modulus. A separate twist $D_0(1/2)$ would give modulus $a^{r/2}$; the shell proof does not extend to $j=-1$. At residual rank zero with positive order $i=N$, there is no admissible higher $h'$. If the higher-order premise were dropped, the actual highest lines in both examples would give equality. A zero derivative has no nonzero subquotient, even at an order below some surviving one.

**Source and scope.** Bernstein's 1984 criterion gives the classical lower-order conclusion in §§7.2–7.4, pp. 81–83, using smooth irreducibility of the original $G_N$-module, the positive definite invariant form, and homogeneity and finite length for the restricted mirabolic module. His pairing proportionality is Theorem A and §5.3, pp. 73–75; the paper's Theorem B concerns distributions. Section 9.4, pp. 98–100, proves the strict condition by shell integrability: the positive eigenfunction modulo compactly supported terms has a nonzero geometric tail when its eigenvalue modulus is at least one. Here the formal argument uses expanding-ball averages and the zero atom on the same representation. A Bernstein–Zelevinsky normalized derivative contributes $\nu^{-i/2}$ relative to $D_i$ by one inverse half modulus per column quotient. Bernstein then multiplies by $\nu^{1/2}$, giving the shift $(1-i)/2$. His exponent statement is phrased for irreducible subquotients. A nonzero subquotient with a scalar action has the same scalar on a simple quotient of any nonzero cyclic submodule. Such a quotient exists since a chain of proper submodules of a cyclic module has proper union. Taking the preimage of the cyclic submodule under the original subquotient map realizes this simple quotient as a subquotient of the derivative. Thus the classical result already covers the spectral assertion for these subquotients. [Bernstein, Theorem A and §§5, 7, 9](https://www.math.tau.ac.il/~bernstei/Publication_list/publication_texts/Bernstein-P-invar-SLN.pdf) · [Bernstein–Zelevinsky I](https://www.numdam.org/item/10.24033/asens.1333/)

The [source and consumer record](#lower-derivative-form-and-shell-supply) identifies Alpöge and Claude's `derivPos_of` and its local helpers. The `UAdm` argument that `uAsm` passes separately goes to `DerivTop`. The lower branch takes `UEig`, whose concrete producer calls `uAdm` itself. The product proof consumes the resulting `DerivPos` through its factor estimate in the positive non-highest branch. The highest-order modulus and both product assemblies retain their cited scope. The assembly and the local strict shell estimate are author-level R3 readings. The full atom and splitting construction, density and lift construction, derivative admissibility's deeper supplies and the production of `ThmB` retain their named interfaces.

### 7.4.4 Top-level P17 $\to$ P7 $\to$ P4 assembly (`HypsAlt` $\to$ `TopG` $\to$ `EveryCharacter`)

Finally, we connect `P17` all the way back to Part I (`EveryCharacter`) by reading the four short
assembly files `SiegelEisensteinSecondTermHypotheses.lean`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopShape.lean`, `DesWin.lean`, and
`TopPlug.lean` in full (`R3` / `[Lean, read]`).

**Theorem 7.4 (From definite-unitary theta hypotheses and `VWin` to `TopG` and `EveryCharacter`).**
*Assume the seven packages:*
- *`P : UnitaryGroupThirdPlaceHypotheses` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/BernsteinUnitaryProductIrreducible.lean:22`),*
- *`F : ThetaLocalInputs`,*
- *`hA : ThetaIntegralsAgree` and `hM : LocalMatching` (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/BlockTheory/DivisorBase/WeilTensorModels.lean:585,581`),*
- *`hX : CovolumeBoundOfX` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/LiftLambdaBound.lean:60`),*
- *`hL : RecTExists` (partial local Langlands, node `P15`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/Foundations/LocalLanglands/IsRecTOfIsRec.lean:78`), and*
- *`hV : VWin` (the Tate–Shafarevich window bound, node `P12`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/ExistsPageN.lean:49`).*

*Then `Nonempty (HypsAlt (fun 𝒞 => Closed 𝒞 fun Fm _ => LinkN Fm) DefiniteSideCondition)` holds,
`TopG` holds, `ZeroFreeAll` holds, and `UpperTheory.EveryCharacter` holds!*
*(Lean: `nonempty_hyps_definite_side_of_closed_bound` and
`every_character_of_theta_integrals_local_matching`,
`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/SiegelEisensteinSecondTermHypotheses.lean:44,69`;
`shape_closed_link_n_of_green`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopShape.lean:33`;
`topDesWin`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/DesWin.lean:11`;
`zero_free_all_of_green_v_win` and `every_character_of_green_v_win`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopPlug.lean:12,16`.)* [Lean, read]

*Proof.* Follow the four-stage wiring diagram in the Lean proof terms:

1. **Building `HypsAlt` (`nonempty_hyps_definite_side_of_closed_bound`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/SiegelEisensteinSecondTermHypotheses.lean:44–68`):**
   - Unramified Howe duality (`howeUnram_holds`) + convergent unitary Siegel–Weil (`P.siegelWeilUnitaryConvergent`)
     imply separation of functionals (`hS`, `:48`).
   - Combining `hS` with real Howe duality (`F.howeDualityReal`), cuspidal Green characters (`P.greenCuspidal`),
     the Hecke–Tate functional equation (`C.fe`), `ThetaIntegralsAgree` (`hA`), and `LocalMatching` (`hM`)
     assembles rational row test vectors (`hRat : S0.RowRat`, `:49`), fits the constant function
     (`hFit : S0.ConstantFunctionFits`, `:51`), and produces an evaluation-grid threshold `CF1` (`evaluation_grid_of_join`, `:52`).
   - Discrete-series covers, Harish-Chandra modules, cusp rapid decay, Casselman–Wallach, and ray
     class fields (`P.*`) produce a second threshold `CF0` (`rowFun_at_gridA_alt`, `:55`).
   - Taking `max CF0 CF1`, applying partial local Langlands `hL : RecTExists` and `hX : CovolumeBoundOfX`
     to link the covolume/height bundles (`hL3`, `:65`), and invoking `hypotheses_nonempty_of_unramified_exponents_alt`
     (`:68`) inhabits `Nonempty (HypsAlt S0.NL DefiniteSideCondition)` (where `S0.NL` comes from `P.nLink`
     via `boundAtTwoThree_linked_of_recT`, `:73`).
2. **Wiring `HypsAlt` + Green's classification into the nine chain links (`shape_closed_link_n_of_green`,
   `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopShape.lean:33–55`):**
   Given `hGreen : GlobalTheory.GLq.Green` (obtained from `P.greenRegularCharacters` via
   `green_of_regular`, `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Proofs/InertiaEigenvalues/SumRangeEqRoots.lean:225`) and the fields of `HypsAlt`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopShape.lean`
   derives:
   - `hDIV`: the divisibility link `Closed (D3 3 𝒜) ... LinkDIVt` (`:47`, from `link_brest_alt`,
     `wire_sel_alt`, twisted admissibility `hA2`, and spectral decay `hC5`);
   - `hE`: smooth design existence `DesignExists` (`:50`, from `hEx`, `hTu`, class-number formula `hn4`,
     and `hLit`);
   - `closed_linkEta_of_duty` (`LinkEta`), `closed_linkIRR_of_join_count hGreen` (`LinkIRR`),
     `link_alt_alt_alt` (`SmallLevelRankEq`), `hgl.1` (lattice rank `NuFullF ∧ J.MuRank`),
     `closed_linkG_of_duty` (`LinkG`), `link_alt_alt_bis` (`LinkS`), and `hgl.2` (`LinkGLOBAL`),
     feeding all nine into `topDesWin` (`:52–55`)!
3. **Converting the nine links + `HypV` into `FamilyClassConnected` (`topDesWin`, `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/DesWin.lean:11–31`):**
   Intersecting the eventual $\varepsilon$-thresholds `min ε1 ε3` of `DesignExists` and the nine
   `Closed` links produces, for every $0 < \varepsilon < \min(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_3)$, a family
   `Fm : FamP eps` in `G.cls la`; combining `HypV` (supplied by `VWin`!) with `LinkN` and
   `SmallLevelRankEq` via `divinN_of_hypV` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/DivinNOfHypV.lean:117`, node `P11`, Chapter 10) discharges
   the congruence-to-$Ш^1$ hypothesis of `aD` (`LinkDIVt`), and `aGL` (`LinkGLOBAL`) outputs
   `ChainTied Fm eD eL ...` across `Fm.Win`, establishing `TopG`!
4. **Closing to `EveryCharacter` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopPlug.lean:12–18`):**
   `zero_free_all_of_green_v_win` plugs `TopG` and `fun ψ => supplyChi ψ (hV ψ)` (Theorem 4.9, at
   window exponent `la := 3 / 2`) into `zeroFreeAll_of_topG` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/ZeroFreeAllOfTopG.lean:26`, Theorem 3.7
   + Corollary 5.6), yielding `ZeroFreeAll`, and `imprimitive` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/Imprimitive.lean:5`, Proposition 2.2)
   extends zero-freeness on $\Re s > 1/2$ to every Dirichlet character (`UpperTheory.EveryCharacter`)! $\square$

## 7.5 Remarks and formalization notes

### 7.5.1 Honest audit of formalization scope in P17: `structure` bundles, `UnusedSlot72`, and unread cones

The [split-place supply for Proposition 7.3f](#split-place-supply-for-proposition-73f) identifies the local representation input separately from the vacuum-span and tensor-matching interfaces.

Reading the actual Lean source of node `P17` brings two essential realities into sharp focus [Expository]:

1. **How hypotheses are packaged vs. discharged:** Inside `SiegelEisensteinSecondTermHypotheses.lean`,
   `LiftConvContGrowth.lean`, `IntegrableThetaKernelProduct.lean`, `ThetaChainUpwardLiftHeckeParamTransferred.lean`,
   and `ThetaChainUpwardInductionFixedMetric.lean` — all of which we read in full and verified free of
   `sorry` (`R3` / `[Lean, read]`) — major representation-theoretic theorems (`CuspRapidDecay`,
   `SiegelWeilUnitaryConvergent`, `CasselmanWallach`, `HoweUnram`, `ThetaIntegralsAgree`, `LocalMatching`,
   `StepUp`) appear **as `structure` fields or explicit theorem hypotheses**! Where are those `Prop`
   fields actually *proved* in the archive? One layer higher up the assembly chain, in
   `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/EveryCharacterOfLiteratureOfVWin.lean:215,810`
   (`P2` / `P18`, import cone **151,285 modules**) [Lean], which proves `every_character_of_literature_of_vWin`
   from `OpenLiteratureStatements` and passes the proved `Prop` terms into
   `every_character_of_theta_integrals_local_matching`!
   - Thus every `GlobalTheory.Published.*` field of `UnitaryGroupThirdPlaceHypotheses` *is* formally
     inhabited somewhere inside the 175,187-file archive with zero axioms beyond Lean's standard three
     `[Lean]`, `[Community]`;
   - **however**, nobody (including us) has read the 151,285-module proof cone behind those `Prop`s,
     and — as noted in `notes/proof-map.md` (node `P18`) and Chapter 13 — whether each custom `Prop`
     definition `GlobalTheory.Published.<Name>` faithfully states the published theorem of that name
     remains `[Open]` until audited declaration by declaration!
2. **A fossil inside `UnitaryGroupThirdPlaceHypotheses` (`UnusedSlot72`):** Look at line 74 of
   `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/BernsteinUnitaryProductIrreducible.lean`:
   ```lean
   siegelEisensteinContinuation : GlobalTheory.Published.SiegelEisensteinContinuation
   siegelEisensteinSimplePole : _root_.GlobalTheory.Published.UnusedSlot72
   schurOrthogonalitySquareIntegrable : GlobalTheory.Published.SchurOrthogonalitySquareIntegrable
   ```
   Field #52, named `siegelEisensteinSimplePole`, has type `GlobalTheory.Published.UnusedSlot72`
   (`:74`) [Lean, read] — and indeed `SiegelEisensteinSecondTermHypotheses.lean` never projects
   `P.siegelEisensteinSimplePole`! Like `ErrorFormsWeakWindowsFalse.lean` in Chapter 5, this unused
   field is harmless to logical soundness (if a hypothesis of an implication is proved — even as `True` —
   and never used, the conclusion is unaffected), while illustrating vividly why human mathematical
   digestion must inspect definitions rather than trusting field names!

### 7.5.2 Structural map of the rest of Part II (Chapters 8–11)

Look back at lines 29–31 of `topDesWin` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/DesWin.lean:11–31`, Step 3 of Theorem 7.4 above):
```lean
have hD := aD hT hDG aE (divinN_of_hypV Fm h0 (hV Fm (h2 Fm hc.1.1)) aN aZ)
exact ⟨Fm, h2 Fm hc.1.1, aGL hT hDG hCh ⟨aG, aS hT, aI, hD, ...⟩⟩
```
That two-line proof term is the **table of contents for Chapters 8–11** [Lean, read]:

- **Chapter 8 (`P8`, Divisibility at inert primes):** Opens `hDIV` / `LinkDIVt` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/ThetaCorrespondence/ThetaPairLifts/ThirdPlaceSeries/TopShape.lean:47`)
  and `WeilJacquetShalikaLaw.Conclusion1` (`src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GrowthBounds/WeilJacquetShalikaLaw.lean:97` [Lean]),
  explaining why at primes of the host inert in $K = \mathbb Q(\sqrt{-d})$, supersingular CM evaluation
  and $p$-adic analysis of the $m$ theta kernels force the columns of the chain to vanish modulo high
  powers of $p$.
- **Chapter 9 (`P9`–`P10`, Heights, slopes, and near-orthogonality):** Opens `SlopeIneq`
  (`src/Formalism/HarderNarasimhan/Statement.lean:91` [Lean]), `aG : LinkG Fm`, `aS hT : LinkS Fm C0`
  (`src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalTheoremInputs.lean:138,152` [Lean]), and `aGL : LinkGLOBAL`,
  showing how Chowla–Selberg periods, Siegel–Minkowski mass ratios (`hU`, `hG`), and Rallis/Bessel
  inner products make distinct chain words nearly orthogonal ($\text{defect} \le \exp(-x^{\varepsilon/8})$)
  and assemble `ChainTied`.
- **Chapter 10 (`P11`, Congruence modules to Tate–Shafarevich groups):** Opens `aN : LinkN Fm`
  (`src/Formalism/SharpRestriction/GlobalTheoremInputs.lean:45` [Lean]), `CutCoreUnit.main`
  (`src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Proofs/InertiaEigenvalues/GlobalCongruenceCountOfRootDifferenceUnit.lean:44` [Lean]),
  and `divinN_of_hypV` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/CharacterBoxes/DivinNOfHypV.lean:117` [Lean]),
  bounding the excess congruence loss of each lift via Fitting ideals on generalized matrix algebras
  in terms of Selmer and $Ш^1\bigl(G_{E,S_\ell}, \mathbb Q_\ell/\mathbb Z_\ell(-e)\bigr)$ lengths.
- **Chapter 11 (`P12`, The window bound `VWin`):** Opens `hV : VWin` (`vwin_of_row`,
  `src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/ModularityArgument/ThresholdArguments/DenseThresholdCases/VwinOfRow.lean:4` [Lean],
  90% of the archive by import cone), splitting $Ш^1$ into $\ell = 2$, minus (Euler systems / Bernoulli
  numbers), and plus ($U(3)$ congruences and residually reducible modularity) parts.

### 7.5.3 Masullo's counterpart

Readers comparing the two November 2026 manuscripts will notice that **Chapter 2 of Masullo's draft
is titled "Adelic $\theta$-functions" (pp. 57–116)** [Paper; Masullo p. 57]. Despite the identical
word, **Masullo's $\theta$-functions are a completely different mathematical object from the
unitary-pair theta kernels of this chapter** (`notes/proof-map.md`, node `P17`) [Expository]:

- In **Section 2.2 ("Global adelic $\theta$-functions", pp. 74–97, specifically Lemma 2.2.2.1 on
  pp. 77–78)**, Masullo associates to a one-variable Schwartz–Bruhat function $f \in \mathcal S(\mathbb A_F)$
  on the adele ring of a number field $F$ its multiplicative $F^\times$-orbit sum on the idele group
  $\mathrm{GL}_1(\mathbb A_F)$ [Paper; Masullo pp. 74, 77–78]:
  $$\Psi_F(f)(g, x) := \sum_{\xi \in F^\times} f\bigl(g(\xi + x)\bigr), \qquad (g, x) \in \mathrm{GL}_1(\mathbb A_F) \times \mathbb A_F,$$
  calling the $F^\times$-invariant function $g \mapsto \Psi_F(f)(g, 0) = \sum_{\xi \in F^\times} f(g\xi)$
  on the idele class group $C_F = \mathrm{GL}_1(\mathbb A_F) / F^\times$ a "global adelic $\theta$-function"
  (whose global idelic Mellin transform in his Chapter 3, pp. 122–127, produces the Hecke $L$-functions
  of Tate's thesis `[Tate1967]`) [Paper; Masullo pp. 74–78, 122–127].
- Notice what is **absent** in Masullo's Chapter 2: there is no symplectic space $W$, no quadratic or
  Hermitian norm form $\langle \xi, \xi \rangle$ in the exponent (his sum is linear in $\xi \in F^\times$,
  corresponding in classical terms to the degenerate hyperbolic dual pair $(\mathrm{GL}_1, \mathrm{GL}_1)$
  inside $\mathrm{Sp}_2$, i.e. ordinary Tate–Poisson summation $\sum_{\xi \in F} f(g\xi) = \lvert g \rvert_F^{-1}\sum_{\xi \in F} \widehat{f}(g^{-1}\xi)$!),
  no higher-rank unitary groups $U(X_j)$, no Howe correspondence, and no chain of lifts!

Speh and the product, determinant, twist and Frobenius rules are at `src/Development/AbelianVarietyTheory/Definitions/GaloisGeometry/ExistsReadingLaws.lean:83–103`.

The interface takes a general unitary-product hypothesis. The proof term ignores that argument and proves the needed special case from the geometric lemma, Bernstein–Zelevinsky finiteness and Zelevinsky polynomiality.

Its further inputs `geometricLemmaFiltration_unconditional`, `BernsteinZelevinskyFiniteness`, `zelevinskyPolynomialRing_of_parts`, `ss`, `cellone`, `detjac`, `nolow`, Jacquet exactness and support transfer are outside this subsection's read upgrade. The full decomposition into ordinary and twisted parts uses the named `UAtom`, `UDens`, `USplit` and `ULift` constructions. The relative admissibility and the pairing proportionality retain their named inputs. The wrapper `uAsm` feeds `UAdm` to `DerivTop` and `UEig` to `DerivPos`. The explicit call through `uEig` uses `uAdm`. Source reading is at author level. Kernel replay, declaration-level Main reachability and external peer review are pending.

**Summary of the chapter.** Classical Poisson summation (Lemma 7.1) endows lattice sums of Schwartz
functions under the Weil representation of a reductive dual pair $(U(V), U(V')) \subset \mathrm{Sp}(W)$
with two commuting automorphic invariance groups `[Jacobi1829]`, `[Weil1964]`, `[Howe1979]` [Expository].
Under explicit sigma-finiteness and Tonelli $L^1$-domination hypotheses — satisfied automatically on
compact definite unitary quotients $[U(X_j)]$ and via moderate-growth $\times$ rapid-decay cancellation
on non-compact quotients (`lift_conv_cont_growth`, Theorem 7.2) [Lean, read] — iterating $m$ bilinear
theta lifts composes their kernels and equates the $m$-step upward inner product $\langle y, x_m \rangle$
to the downward seesaw pairing $\langle \Theta y, u \rangle$ while adjoining one non-interlacing
spectral block $\mu_j[t]$ per step (`HCIn.hc`, `thetaChain_upward_induction_fixed_metric`,
Proposition 7.3) [Lean, read]. In node `P17`, `nonempty_hyps_definite_side_of_closed_bound`,
`shape_closed_link_n_of_green`, `topDesWin`, and `every_character_of_green_v_win` (Theorem 7.4) [Lean, read]
assemble the definite-unitary chain families `HypsAlt` from 60 named literature `Prop` slots
(`UnitaryGroupThirdPlaceHypotheses`, audited honestly in Section 7.5.1) and wire them to `TopG` and
`EveryCharacter`, leaving four concrete arithmetic links — inert-prime divisibility (`P8`), slopes and
near-orthogonality (`P9`–`P10`), congruence modules (`P11`), and `VWin` (`P12`) — for Chapters 8–11.
